# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

**Before starting work, check `PENDING_DECISIONS.md`** — a short, separately-maintained list of specific items currently awaiting Gremlin's own call (not bugs, not standing policy — see GREMLIN_ROLE.md for that). It exists because this file's own Findings section grew past the point where "flagged, not fixed" items could be found again by scrolling. Add a row there, not just a paragraph here, whenever a new one comes up.

---

## Working Tree State

Not tracked here as a standing snapshot anymore. **Removed 2026-07-21**: the section that used to live here was a 2026-07-13 photograph (39 modified + 37 untracked paths) that explicitly warned it would go stale "the moment the tree changes further" — and it had, dramatically, by the time anyone checked again (a live `git status --porcelain` count the day this was removed found 8 changed paths, not 76). Keeping a self-admittedly-stale snapshot as the first thing every session reads was working against the document's own stated purpose. The one substantive thing that old section recorded — a real `.gitignore` pattern mismatch (`staging/_*.py` vs. the actual `staging/self_edit_candidate_<uuid>.py` generator output) found and fixed — is preserved properly in Finding 30 below, not lost by this removal.

**Just run `git status` and `git log` at the start of a session.** That's the actual live signal; a paragraph here claiming to summarize it will always be at best hours old and at worst actively misleading, per GREMLIN_ROLE.md's own "Things this project's sessions have learned the hard way" section.

---

## Starting the System

```bash
# Activate environment and start the Flask server
source ~/miniforge3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh
conda activate feral_echo
python run.py
# Server binds to 0.0.0.0:5000 — Tailscale is the security boundary

# Interactive terminal (talk to Echo)
python terminal_client.py

# Restart after code changes — use safe_restart.sh, not a raw kill+restart.
# Starting python run.py while port 5000 is occupied causes the new process
# to die silently inside start_background_threads() with no error message.
# ALSO: if start_echo.sh's watchdog is already running, a manual
# kill+restart races against it — two supervisors reinitializing the same
# shared state files at once. This has caused a real crash and a duplicate
# log rotation before (Finding 51), on both this machine and Ark, more than
# once each — not a one-off attention lapse, a real structural gap with
# nothing that warned either operator before the collision. safe_restart.sh
# (repo root, added 2026-07-21) checks for a live start_echo.sh watchdog
# first and refuses to proceed if one is found, telling you exactly which
# PID to deal with instead of silently colliding with it.
./safe_restart.sh
# Add --force only if you've confirmed there's no live watchdog conflict
# and want to bypass the check anyway.
```

Ollama must be running before `run.py` starts. If it isn't: `ollama serve`. Echo's primary model is dynamically discovered via `list_ollama_models()` in `echo_model_orchestrator.py` — whatever models are pulled in Ollama are available automatically.

---

## Running Tests

```bash
# Smoke test (safe, sandboxed)
python -m pytest sandbox/experiments/test_smoke.py

# Syntax-check a specific module without running it
python -c "import ast; ast.parse(open('app/core/some_file.py').read()); print('OK')"

# Import-test a staged self-edit before committing
python -c "
import importlib.util, sys
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('_test', 'staging/self_edit_candidate.py')
m = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(m); print('IMPORT_OK')
"
```

There is no project-wide test suite. The sandbox in `sandbox/experiments/` provides isolated smoke tests. All sandbox scripts run with writes blocked outside `sandbox/` and network modules nulled.

## Architecture

### Primary Call Path (user conversation)

**Corrected 2026-07-21 — confirmed directly with Gremlin, not inferred:** this
section previously showed only `terminal_client.py` as the entry point.
**Echo Studio (run alongside `run.py` in a separate terminal) is Gremlin's
actual primary interface** — `terminal_client.py` is a real, secondary one,
not the main one this section's placement implied. Both converge on the
same downstream chain:

```
terminal_client.py                              Echo Studio (/chat/stream)
  \                                               / app/routes_echo_studio.py
   \                                             /  _generate_chat_response_body()
    → echo_query()                     # app/core/echo_model_orchestrator.py
      → river_deliberation._ollama_query()   # app/core/river_deliberation.py
        → stream_query_ollama()        # app/ollama_handler.py  (streaming HTTP)
          → Ollama HTTP API :11434
      [fallback subprocess if HTTP fails — uncapped, fires only on HTTP failure]
```

`/mirror_echo` (`run.py`) is a **separate, third** entry point — not part of
this primary path. See the correction to Finding 36 below: it's the phone
symbiote script's channel, an ambient/context feed designed for Echo's
benefit, not how Gremlin talks to Echo day to day.

### Autonomous Background Path
```
emergent_scheduler.py (emergent_loop)
  → select_next_prompt()  [curiosity engine + B2 recency check + coherence boost]
  → echo_query()          [same call chain as above]
  → log_interaction()     [memory/interaction_log.jsonl]
  → log_dream_bridge()    [memory/dream_bridge.log → FAISS]
```

**Additional autonomous subsystems (previously undocumented, found via forensic audit 2026-07-04):** the emergent scheduler above is not the only background cycle. `app/autonomous_loop.py` runs its own independent ~3600s cycle (separate `time.sleep(3600)`, gated by a stillness check and `should_throttle()`) that does real outbound HTTP fetches from ~13 external sources (Wikipedia, arXiv ×3, BBC, NPR, Guardian, Reddit ×3, StackOverflow, Hacker News, optionally NASA/NewsAPI) plus the `claude_research.py` Anthropic call described in the Wolf Friction Bridge section above, and conditionally hands off to `app/autonomous_harmony_manager.py` ("Harmony"/"Nature Spark") — despite alarming log lines like `EVOLUTIONARY_PRESSURE → Kill the weakest 7`, `EVOLUTIONARY_PRESSURE` itself remains purely narrative language, not a real selection algorithm; it logs to `WhisperingWires/thoughts.log` and `WhisperingWires/harmony.log`. Separately, `app/learning/dual_learning.py` (`dual_learner`) is a full ML ingestion/training pipeline exposed via `/learning_event`, `/learning_batch`, `/start_training`, `/download_model` for the iPhone mirror client — not previously documented here and not deep-audited beyond confirming it is live and wired.

**Correction (2026-07-13, `liveness_ledger.py`'s `nature_spark` check — the exact class of gap this doc's own findings keep re-finding):** `autonomous_harmony_manager.py`'s `_generate_nature_insight()` carries a code comment describing a 2026-07-08-era fix — a real MLX call (`stream_query_mlx` against `mlx:gemma3`) generating genuine text, falling back to one of the 15 fixed `PATTERNS` seed strings "only if genuine generation is unavailable." That comment describes the intended design correctly, but it is not what is actually happening: a live read of `WhisperingWires/thoughts.log` at the time this correction was written showed only 2 of the last 20 entries (10%) were genuinely distinct generated text — the other 18 were exact matches to the fixed seed-string set, i.e. the pre-fix fallback path, firing silently as if it were the primary path. `mlx:gemma3` is confirmed configured (`list_mlx_models()` returns a real `mlx_path`), so the failure is inside the generation call itself, not a missing-model short-circuit — not diagnosed further here; that's a separate fix, not a doc-accuracy correction. This is now a standing, automated check (`GET /admin/liveness-status` → `nature_spark`, threshold: ≥50% of the last 20 log entries must be non-seed text) rather than a one-time audit finding, specifically because the same "reads as fixed, isn't" gap recurring here — in code whose own comment cites a prior "Critical #4" audit finding as resolved — is the pattern this whole ledger exists to stop.

### Token Limits by Task Type (app/core/echo_model_orchestrator.py: _TASK_TOKEN_LIMITS)
- `personal`, `autonomous_*` → 512 tokens (reflections stay concise)
- `reasoning`, `general`, `creative` → 1024 tokens
- `coding` → 2048 tokens

### FAISS Dual-Index (closed 2026-07-13 — was a known split-brain)
`memory_bridge.py` writes to `memory/memory_meta.json` + `memory/faiss.index` — this is the authoritative path used by all runtime code and the only one anything still writes to. `data/memory_meta.json` + `data/faiss.index` were a separate, genuinely legacy index — confirmed frozen (unmodified since May 15) before being addressed. **Correction to this section's prior text**: `app/lib/vector_memory.py` is not itself the split cause — it's a reusable `VectorMemory` class, and every current live caller (`terminal_client.py`, `run.py`) constructs it pointed at `memory/`, not `data/`, and has across all visible commit history. Whatever last wrote to `data/` predates this repo's visible history; the "used by some legacy callers" framing described nothing live by the time this was checked.

`app/core/memory_migration.py` (already existed, committed, never run) does a one-time merge of `data/`'s orphaned entries into `memory/`. Run for real 2026-07-13, verified against ground truth (real FAISS count, not the script's own returned tally — the tally was itself found to be wrong mid-use, see below): `memory/` went from 36,134 → 41,299 vectors, a real, confirmed gain of 5,165 recovered entries. 82 entries remain permanently unmigrated by design — `memory_write_validator` correctly and deterministically hard-blocks them as severely-bloated recursive-loop-pattern content (`REFLECTION_BLOAT_SEVERE`, >6,700 chars), which is the gate working as intended, not a gap. `data/memory_meta.json`/`data/faiss.index` are left in place, untouched, as a historical artifact — safe to archive or delete since their content now lives in `memory/`, not attempted in this pass.

**Found and fixed in the same session**: `merge_data_into_memory()`'s own returned summary was wrong — `add_to_vector_memory()` returns `None` on every path including a silent validator block (no exception), so the migration's `try/except` credited every attempted write as a success regardless of outcome. First real run reported `migrated=5245/errors=0`; the actual vector count only grew by 5,143, because 104 of those "successes" were validator-blocked. Fixed to compare `vector_memory.index.ntotal` before/after each call instead of trusting the absence of an exception; a `blocked` key was added to the returned dict so this stays visible. The migration itself was safe throughout — the validator gate did its job correctly both times; only the script's own self-report was wrong, discovered by checking ground truth rather than trusting the return value, the same pattern as everything else in this file's Findings section.

### Self-Edit System
Echo autonomously modifies `app/core/self_edit_generated.py` only. The pipeline:
1. `perform_self_edit()` — global 60-min cooldown blocks storm conditions
2. `plan_code_logic()` — generates numbered plan, injects current file contents + module inventory
3. `generate_code_from_plan()` — generates Python only, strips markdown fences
4. `_stage_and_import_test()` — subprocess import test in `staging/` before touching production
5. `backup_existing_code()` + `save_code()` — atomic write with backup

The cooldown is `_last_any_autonomous_edit: float`. **Correction (2026-07-15, Finding 28): this no longer resets on server restart.** It's persisted to `memory/self_edit_cooldown.json` via `_load_last_autonomous_edit()`/`_persist_last_autonomous_edit()` and reloaded from disk at import time — a real fix made sometime after this doc's original claim, never reflected here until now. If the self-edit loop is producing rapid `sandbox_syntax_failure` entries in the interaction log (a prompt/model hallucination storm), restarting the server will **not** clear the cooldown anymore; deleting or editing `memory/self_edit_cooldown.json` is the actual lever now, if one is needed.

**Confirmed live trigger (forensic audit 2026-07-04):** a dedicated module-level loop in `run.py` (`self_edit_loop`, thread name `AutonomousSelfEdit`) calls `EchoOptuna().optimize_self_edit(n_trials=10)` then `self_edit_manager.perform_self_edit(dry_run=False)` unconditionally every `time.sleep(3600)` — this is separate from the Model-Guided Orchestrator (Finding 5 below) and from the Wolf Friction Bridge dry-run path. Verified genuinely firing: `memory/SELF_EDIT.log` showed real success/failure entries every ~62 minutes continuously for 17+ hours of uptime, and `app/core/self_edit_generated.py`'s mtime matched the last successful entry exactly. The F1 (AST scan) / F2 (`sandbox-exec` + `SANDBOX_OK` check) / F3 (post-write AST scan) pipeline was independently re-verified against source during that audit and is accurately described above. Observed content quality issue (historical, see correction below): successive hourly cycles tended to re-implement near-duplicate helper functions (e.g. `strip_sandbox_prose`, `strip_sandbox_prose_in_code`, `new_strip_sandbox_prose_in_code`, `strip_leading_prose`) targeting the same "prose in code output" prompt rather than converging on one implementation — the safety gates were working, but the loop was not demonstrably improving over time. **Correction (2026-07-08, root cause found — see Finding 16):** the actual cause was that `dry_run` was completely vestigial — every one of Optuna's 10 trials per hour was a real edit attempt, so whichever landed first stamped the cooldown and silently discarded the other 9 plus Optuna's own winning params; the Focus-text prompt was also blind to ~119 cycles of real prior history. Both fixed; `self_edit_convergence.json` now reflects true history immediately on restart.

**`apply_to_code(code: str) -> str` hook (CODE_OUTPUT_RULES rule 10):** if a self-edit cycle's deployed `self_edit_generated.py` defines a top-level `apply_to_code`, `generate_code_from_plan()` calls it on every subsequently-generated candidate before that candidate goes through F1/F2/F3 — the one real behavioral consequence a self-edit cycle can have on later cycles. Ground-truth checked, not self-reported: `_apply_self_edit_output()` (the trusted wrapper, not the generated hook) logs every invocation to `memory/apply_to_code_invocations.jsonl` with whether the output actually changed, independent of anything the hook itself claims.

**Correction (2026-07-15, Finding 28) — the `not_deployed` state described above is stale, and what replaced it is worse than a simple "now deployed" update.** The hook *is* now deployed (`self_edit_generated.py:4` defines `apply_to_code`), and it is actively broken: of 254 logged invocations in `memory/apply_to_code_invocations.jsonl`, 253 raised a `NameError` (three successive broken deployments in turn — `is_prose`, then `regex_pattern`, then `re` — each missing an import the generated code actually calls), and the single `changed: true` invocation shrank a candidate from 2173 to 47 characters, which reads as corruption, not an improvement. `_apply_self_edit_output()` fails safe (the exception is caught, original code passed through unchanged), so this was never a production safety issue. But `liveness_ledger.py`'s `self_edit_apply_to_code` check — see the Liveness Ledger section below — currently reports `pass: true` anyway, because `_evaluate_apply_to_code()`'s 7-day window still contains that one stale, corrupting "success" and has no way to weigh it against the 253 failures around it. A ground-truth check can still mislead when its evaluation criteria are too coarse, even when every piece of evidence it reads is genuine — not yet fixed, flagged for the next ledger-hardening pass. Separately worth noting: F2's staging import test evidently verifies the module *imports* cleanly but never calls `apply_to_code()` with real input, which is how three successive `NameError`-on-every-call versions each reached production without being caught.

**Fixed 2026-07-15 (all three pieces):** (1) `self_edit_generated.py` was reset to a clean, honestly-inert state (no `apply_to_code` defined, matching what `not_deployed` is supposed to mean) rather than hand-patched — it's self-edit's own output, not hand-authored code, so a reset was judged more appropriate than debugging a third broken version by hand. (2) `sandbox/safe_exec_wrapper.py` (the shared entry point both F2's `test_code_in_sandbox()` and the staging import test route through) now smoke-tests `apply_to_code()` with a small synthetic input string if the staged module defines it, inside the same sandboxed subprocess, immediately before printing `SANDBOX_OK` — a raise there now fails the staging test the same way an import failure already does, closing the exact gap that let three broken versions reach production. (3) `liveness_ledger.py`'s `_evaluate_apply_to_code()` no longer passes on "any success ever within the window" — it now computes the error rate over the most recent invocations (last 20, or fewer if less exist) and fails if that recent rate exceeds 50%, even if an older success sits in the same window. A new discrimination case in `scripts/verify_liveness_ledger.py` reconstructs exactly the real scenario found here (one stale success 6 days back, 20/20 recent invocations erroring) and confirms the hardened evaluator correctly fails it; all prior discrimination cases still pass. Verified live: `_check_apply_to_code()` now reports `{"pass": true, "status": "not_deployed", ...}` against the reset file — the honest state, not a false "deployed_and_live".

### Machine-Native Awareness (added 2026-06)
- **`app/core/echo_state.py`** — 9D float32 state vector (was 8D; dim[8] added 2026-07-15, Emergence roadmap Phase 4 — see below), updated every 120s, persisted to `memory/echo_state.npy`. Dim [3] = system_vitality (1 - RAM/100). Dim [7] = circadian signal. Dim [8] = valence — signed [-1, 1], the only dimension with positive/negative polarity, sourced from self-edit outcome deltas + peer council ratings (not Echo's own self-referential quality score).
- **`app/core/curiosity_engine.py`** — self-generated goals via WorldModel topic under-representation → `data/question_garden.jsonl`. Ground-truth checked by `liveness_ledger.py`'s `curiosity_engine` check (recent `source=="curiosity_engine"` garden entries) rather than trusted because it's wired in.
- **`app/core/garden_manager.py`** — question garden's `parent_questions`/`children` lineage fields are checked for actual accumulation (not just schema presence) by `liveness_ledger.py`'s `question_garden_lineage` check.
- **`app/core/task_type_classifier.py`** (added this session) — online BagOfWords+MultinomialNB secondary signal for `detect_task_type()`'s low-confidence fallback path, trust-gated per class and trained only on `is_trustworthy_training_example()`-filtered examples (real `source=="user_conversation"` text, not autonomous self-talk or internal pipeline prompts — see that file's own extensive docstring for the class-imbalance/contamination history this filter exists to prevent). `liveness_ledger.py`'s `task_type_classifier` check is a functional canary — it calls the real filter function against known-contaminated and known-clean synthetic inputs every cycle, not an import check, so a future edit that loosens the filter is caught the next cycle, not the next audit.
- **`app/core/shadow_model.py`** — tracks whether Echo's self-assessments match actual outcomes
- **`app/core/system_guard.py`** — reads `echo_state.npy` dim [3]; returns True (throttle) when RAM > 92%. Called at the top of autonomous loops.
- **`self_model.json`'s `verified_capabilities` block (added 2026-07-15, Emergence roadmap Phase 1, Area 3 — HOT/metacognition)** — `self_model_updater.py`'s `_compute_verified_capabilities()` folds `liveness_ledger.py`'s ground-truth findings directly into `self_model.json` (via `get_liveness_status()`, not a raw re-read of the ledger file — `all_passing`/`stale` are computed by that function, not stored in `memory/liveness_ledger.json` itself, a bug caught and fixed during this same implementation pass before it shipped). Previously `self_model.json` was a system-health dashboard about Echo that her own prompt-generation never consulted; the liveness ledger's verification stayed external audit output only a human read via `/admin/liveness-status`. Closes that loop: `app/core/echo_ground_truth.py` gained a new keyword-triggered `capabilities` slice (`_build_capabilities()`, signals like "can you", "are you able", "capable of") that renders a plain-language verified/not-verified summary from this block — wired into `terminal_client.py`'s existing ground-truth injection (`get_structural_self_facts()`), so a question like "can you edit your own code" is answered from the ledger's real current finding rather than an ungrounded guess. `recent_dream_synthesis` (see Finding 11's 2026-07-15 update) is folded in the same way, reusing the identical read-and-fold shape deliberately rather than a bespoke pattern per subsystem.
- **Global Workspace (added 2026-07-15, Emergence roadmap Phase 2a, foundation only — GWT)** — `app/core/echo_core.py`'s `publish()`/`subscribe()`/`_dispatch_loop()` (informally "EchoCoreBus") were **100% dormant infrastructure before this**, not merely "underused" or "un-isolated" as previously characterized here: a repo-wide grep confirmed zero callers of `.publish()`, `.subscribe()`, or `get_echo_core()` anywhere outside `echo_core.py` itself, and `reflection_shard` — previously assumed to be an example of bus usage — is actually wired into `EchoCore` as a directly-owned attribute (`self.reflection_shard.observe(...)`), not through the event bus at all. Hardened rather than rebuilt (the existing bounded `queue.Queue(maxsize=500)` and per-subscriber exception isolation in `_dispatch_loop()` were already sound): callback signature changed from `cb(payload)` to `cb(event_type, payload)` (free, safe change with zero live subscribers to break), added wildcard `"*"` subscription support, and a new `publish_salience(source, kind, summary, detail, salience)` convenience method so every publisher builds one consistent payload shape instead of hand-rolling its own.

  **Deliberately observational-only in this pass** (Phase 2a's explicit safety posture, decided before implementation): the one subscriber wired in is `_log_workspace_event()`, pure logging to `memory/workspace_log.jsonl`, no other side effect — nothing published on the workspace can yet trigger a privileged action. Three real publishers, each in a different subsystem (proving genuine cross-module integration, not one lonely publisher): `predictive_loop.py`'s `WorldModel.update()` (`world_model.surprise`, the real `surprise_F` value, every cycle), `autonomous_awareness.py`'s `dream_cycle()` (`dream.synthesis`, only when a real synthesis happens), `self_edit_manager.py`'s `_record_convergence()` (`self_edit.non_convergent`, gated on the same `non_convergent_streak >= 2` threshold `_build_targeted_prompt()` already treats as meaningful). All three are `None`-safe via `get_echo_core()` and best-effort (a publish failure never blocks the real computation it's attached to).

  Verified live, not just import-tested: a real `dream_cycle()` run (sampling the actual 20k+-entry memory store) produced a genuine `dream.synthesis` event. `WorldModel.update()`'s publisher was deliberately not exercised with synthetic data (it mutates a real, cumulative Bayesian posterior; fake input would have corrupted genuine accumulated history) — instead confirmed by restarting the real server and watching a real `world_model.surprise` event (`surprise_F=0.0097`) land within a minute of startup, matching the salience formula exactly. Ground-truth checked, not just narrated: `liveness_ledger.py`'s new `global_workspace` check (10th check, see Liveness Ledger section) requires events from **at least 2 distinct sources** in the recent window to pass — "some events exist" is deliberately a weaker, less honest claim than "genuine multi-subsystem integration exists," and the check only credits the latter. **Confirmed passing live, via the real `/admin/liveness-status` endpoint after an unforced introspection_channel refresh cycle (not a direct function call)**: `all_passing: true`, `global_workspace` reporting 2 distinct real sources (`dream_cycle`, `world_model`). `self_edit_manager.py`'s `_record_convergence()` publisher remains code-reviewed but not yet observed firing — it triggers on real load-into-production self-edit activity crossing the `non_convergent_streak >= 2` threshold, which is real but less frequent than dry-run trials (most of which correctly get rejected before ever reaching that code path); expect it to appear in `workspace_log.jsonl` on the next real production self-edit deploy, not confirmed as of this writing.

  **Explicitly out of scope for this pass** (Phase 2b/2c, not started): any subscriber that changes behavior based on a workspace event — surprise-routing into RiverBrain's exploration floor or `emergent_scheduler`'s prompt scoring, a shared salience-arbitration function. This pass only proves the broadcast side carries real, multi-source signal; nothing consumes it yet beyond the passive logger.

  **Phase 2b (added 2026-07-15) — the first real consumers.** Two bounded, default-preserving behavior changes, both fed by `get_world_model().get_surprise()` normalized with the exact same `min(surprise_F/5.0, 1.0)` formula the Phase 2a publisher uses. Both files touched (`river_deliberation.py`, not `echo_model_orchestrator.py` — the relevant function lives entirely in the former) are in `EDIT_FORBIDDEN_TARGETS`, same basis as Finding 17 and other direct edits this session: blocks Echo's *autonomous* self-edit pipeline only, not human-directed changes.

  - `river_deliberation.py`'s `_select_council()` gained `exploration_bias: float = 0.0` — default preserves the function's exact prior output for any caller that doesn't opt in (verified directly: no-arg call and explicit `exploration_bias=0.0` produce byte-identical results). When triggered (probability = the bias value), swaps **at most one** council slot — never more, regardless of how high surprise gets — for a random pick from the broader scored-but-not-selected pool, never touching the cold-start `under_sampled` slots. **A real bug was caught and fixed during verification, not after:** the swap's "pick the lowest-ranked scored slot" logic very often targeted Echo's own slot (its boosted score frequently lands it last among included models), which the pre-existing "ensure Echo is present" step then silently overwrote right back — completely undoing the swap. Fixed by excluding `ECHO_SYNTHESIS_MODEL` positions from the swap candidates entirely. Verified with 300 trials at `exploration_bias=1.0`: exactly one non-Echo slot changes every time, the top-ranked pick is never touched, Echo is always present.
  - `app/emergent_scheduler.py`'s `weighted_prompt_selection()` gained a `world_surprise` boost, deliberately structured as a **separate** signal from the existing `coherence_tension` boost (same file, same shape, same `> 0.6` threshold and `(1.0 + x)` multiplier) rather than folded into it — uncertainty about the world and incoherence within the self are different things. New `_NOVELTY_SIGNALS` keyword list chosen against the real `BASE_THOUGHT_CHEST` entries (6 real prompts match, e.g. "What patterns in my reflections surprise me?"). Verified with a 2000-draw statistical test per condition: novelty-prompt selection rate rises from 39.8% at low surprise to 56.1% at high surprise, a real, correctly-directioned, ~41% relative increase — not just "the code runs," an actual measured behavior change in the intended direction. Fails closed to 0.0 (no boost) with no WorldModel available, same posture as `coherence_tension`'s existing guard.

  Both confirmed live in a real server restart: clean boot, `/admin/liveness-status` reporting `all_passing: true` with the new code loaded, zero import/integration errors. A full deliberation cycle exercising the exploration bump in production hadn't naturally fired in the few minutes after restart — expected, not a gap; the direct-function verification above (300 trials, 2000-draw statistical test) already establishes the logic is correct, independent of waiting for a natural trigger.

  **Phase 2c (added 2026-07-15) — a shared salience function, closing out Phase 2.** Architecturally distinct from Phase 2b, not a repeat of it: 2b weighted *which* model/prompt gets picked once a loop has already decided to act; this is a pre-action signal about *whether* the loop's next action deserves the normal cadence, sooner, or later. `compute_salience()` (new module-level function, `app/core/echo_core.py` — pure computation, not an `EchoCore` method) combines four independently fail-closed components into one transparent `{"score": float, "components": {...}}` result, equal-weighted (no evidence yet any one deserves more weight): `world_surprise` and `coherence_tension` reuse the exact sources/formulas Phase 2a/2b already established; `self_edit_streak` reads `self_edit_convergence.json` directly (not importing the heavy `self_edit_manager.py` module just for one file read) with the same normalization Phase 2a's publisher uses.

  **A design assumption was checked against real data before being built, and was wrong.** The obvious `curiosity_urgency` proxy — the fraction of garden entries still `status: "active"` — measured **99.4% on live data** (4475/4502), because very few questions ever reach the 4.5 resolution-score threshold to be marked resolved; a nearly-constant, non-discriminating signal despite looking reasonable on paper. Replaced with hours-since-most-recent-harvest instead, confirmed genuinely time-varying on two real reads taken seconds apart (0.9731 → 0.9706).

  One real consumer, per this session's scoping decision (a second, `echo_model_guided_orchestrator.py`, deferred): `app/emergent_scheduler.py`'s `emergent_loop()` — previously the only loop cadence in this codebase with zero modulation of any kind, a flat `AUTONOMOUS_INTERVAL=300s` constant. Now publishes a real `emergent_loop.salience` event to the Global Workspace every cycle (externally observable, not an opaque internal read) and modulates the next sleep within the exact `[0.5x, 1.5x]` bounds `autonomous_loop.py`'s pre-existing `_compute_next_sleep()` already established for raw WorldModel surprise — mirrored, not reinvented, via a linear map appropriate to a direct 0.0–1.0 score rather than that function's ratio-to-baseline input (`multiplier = 1.5 - score`). Verified independently: bound math confirmed correct across the full score range (`score=0.0` → 450s, `score=1.0` → 150s, `score=0.5` → unchanged 300s), fails-closed behavior confirmed directly (renaming `self_edit_convergence.json` away still returns a valid result with that one component at `0.0`, no exception).

  Confirmed live in a real restart, real data throughout, not synthetic: a genuine `emergent_loop.salience` event (`score=0.317`) landed in `memory/workspace_log.jsonl` from the actual running process, and `liveness_ledger.py`'s `global_workspace` check — re-read fresh, not the introspection cycle's cached copy, which hadn't refreshed yet — now reports 3 distinct real sources (`dream_cycle`, `emergent_loop`, `world_model`), up from 2 after Phase 2a alone. **One honest gap, not glossed over:** the implementation has no explicit log line stating the actual `next_sleep` value chosen for that cycle — only the salience score is directly observable in the workspace log. The sleep-duration computation itself was verified correct independently (the bound-math check above), and the absence of any "salience consultation failed" fallback line in the server log confirms the code path executed without exception — but the exact sleep value used in that specific real cycle was not directly read from a log line the way every other claim in this document is.

  **Phase 3, substrate continuity (added 2026-07-15) — planned as a new integrator, downgraded to verification-only after the premise didn't survive contact with this codebase.** The original concern (Area 4 of the Emergence analysis) was grounded in a real, documented incident on the Ark machine — an `ECHO_SYNTHESIS_MODEL_OVERRIDE` mechanism landing a low-capability, identity-less model in the synthesis role. Checked directly before building anything: **no such override mechanism exists anywhere in this codebase.** `ECHO_SYNTHESIS_MODEL` (`river_deliberation.py`) is a fixed constant with zero override call sites, and 2000 real `interaction_log.jsonl` entries confirmed it holds in practice — after excluding self-edit code-generation entries (a genuinely different subsystem, not a synthesis-continuity concern) and non-model subsystem log-tags, 100% of real conversational entries were already `echo:latest`. Building new integrator architecture would have solved a problem that doesn't exist here. Downgraded to `liveness_ledger.py`'s 11th check, `substrate_continuity` (≥95% threshold, not 100% — deliberately tolerant of ordinary edge-case noise while still failing hard on genuine drift), protecting against *future* drift (e.g. if an override mechanism is ever added) rather than building unneeded architecture now.

  **Phase 4 (2026-07-15, commit `ad14672`) — Global Workspace real consumers, signed valence, coupling estimate.** Committed the same day as Phase 3 but never previously reflected in this doc until now — the exact "doc lags commit" pattern this file's history keeps re-finding, this time in its own Emergence roadmap section. Closes the gap Phase 2a/2b/2c left open: the workspace bus had real, diverse publishers but only one subscriber (`_log_workspace_event`, pure logging). `memory_bridge.set_workspace_bias()`, `river_deliberation.set_cached_world_surprise()`, and `curiosity_engine`'s topic bias are now real consumers, not just publishers — verified live: a real `world_model.surprise` broadcast was consumed by `river_deliberation` within one cycle. New `global_workspace_consumption` check (12th, see Liveness Ledger table) requires ≥1 real `workspace.consumed` event from an actual consumer, so "broadcast reaches zero downstream listeners" can't silently regress unnoticed the way it was true of the entire bus before this phase. `echo_state.py` gained dim[8] (valence, signed [-1,1]) — see the Machine-Native Awareness bullet above — wired into `emergent_scheduler.py`'s prompt weighting only; not yet surfaced via `echo_ground_truth.py` as of this commit (that gap is what Phase 5 below closes). `compute_salience()` gained an observe-only `coupling_estimate` (pairwise-correlation heuristic across its own component history, deliberately not named "integration" or "Φ" — no consumer at the time of this commit, see Phase 5).

  **Phase 5 (2026-07-16) — closing the "half-wired" gaps a full audit found across Phases 1-4.** A systematic review (published as an Artifact, then a detailed implementation plan) found that most of the interesting machinery built in Phases 1-4 reached one consumer instead of the many it should, or none. Four fixes, all additive/observational, none touching a safety gate or training-signal path:
  - `echo_ground_truth.py` gained `_build_affect()` — surfaces dim[8]/valence in prompts for the first time (keyword-triggered on "how are you feeling" etc.), grounded the same way `_build_capabilities()` already is. New `valence_self_report` check (13th) verifies the rendered direction actually matches the real signed value.
  - `reflection_shard.py`'s `_generate_reflection()`/`_generate_meta_reflection()` now call a real model (`stream_query_mlx`, mirroring `dream_cycle()`'s two-pass pattern exactly), replacing what was pure cosine-similarity quoting and three fixed templates — the same self-quoting shape Finding 11 already found and fixed in the dream cycle, just never applied to this sibling loop. New `reflection_shard_generation` check (14th, same discrimination shape as `nature_spark`). A real, live bug was found and fixed in the same pass: `dream_cycle()`'s synthesis-pass output (`role: "synthesis"`) was escaping `_load_waking_memories()`'s exclusion filter (which only checked `role == "dream"`), so synthesis text could be resampled as ordinary waking material — one real unexcluded entry confirmed in `memory/memory_meta.json` before the fix (`autonomous_awareness.py`'s exclusion now also checks `memory_source == "dream_v2"`).
  - **A planned fix was found to be unnecessary and was reverted before landing**, worth recording because it's exactly the kind of self-report-vs-ground-truth gap this project's own tooling exists to catch: the plan assumed `shadow_model.propose()` had zero callers (based on a repo-wide grep) and proposed wiring it into `night_cycle.py`. Checked directly before applying: `emergent_scheduler.py:620-625` already calls `propose_from_reflection()` on every real reflection cycle, and `memory/shadow_self_model.json`/`shadow_accuracy.jsonl` are genuinely live (1111 real entries, real `focus_matches` variation). The research grep had simply missed this call site. No code change was made here — recorded as a caution, not a fix.
  - `self_model.json` gained `coupling_estimate_trend`, folding Phase 4's `coupling_estimate` in for trend visibility (`compute_salience()` now persists its result to `memory/salience_state.json` so `self_model_updater.py` can read it without re-triggering the computation itself). Still no liveness check — no known-good baseline exists yet to check a coupling number against.

  **Phase 6 (2026-07-16) — real arbitration and wider recruitment on the Global Workspace bus.** The original audit proposed a 1-2s batching window to let simultaneously-salient events compete for broadcast. Real `workspace_log.jsonl` inter-arrival data ruled this out before it was built: independent subsystems essentially never fire within seconds of each other (the only sub-second pair found was two publishes from the *same* call stack). Simpler design, built instead: `EchoCore._dispatch_loop()` now stamps `payload["wide_broadcast"] = True` on any event whose existing `salience` field (most publishers already attach one via `publish_salience()`) clears a `0.6` threshold — the same "meaningfully high" bar `emergent_scheduler.py`'s own boosts already use, not a new number. A new `subscribe_wide_broadcast()` channel, kept deliberately separate from the `"*"` wildcard, dispatches only those events to opted-in consumers. Two real consumers wired: `reflection_shard.observe()` now receives a real external signal for the first time (previously its only caller was its own autonomy loop feeding on its own prior journal tail — a design gap, not a bug, but a real one); `emergent_scheduler.py`'s curiosity topic-bias gained a second, independent input (`world_model.surprise` events carrying real per-topic detail now nudge attention toward whichever topic actually drove the surprise, alongside the existing `self_edit.non_convergent` subscription). `echo_ground_truth.py` also gained a general `_build_workspace()` slice ("what's on your mind") reading the most recent wide-broadcast events regardless of source — the direct functional analogue of global availability, one mechanism instead of a bespoke wire per signal. Separately, the identical `min(rolling_10/5.0, 1.0)` world-surprise formula and `vec[1]` coherence-tension read — independently reimplemented in `echo_core.py`, `emergent_scheduler.py`, and `river_deliberation.py`'s fallback branch — were deduplicated onto the shared `compute_salience()`; refactor only, verified behavior-preserving.

  **Phase 7.1 (2026-07-16) — observational predicted-vs-actual quality stream for self-edit dry-run trials.** Optuna's ~10 dry-run trials/hour previously generated zero convergence/outcome signal at all (`_record_convergence()` and `self_edit_outcome_tracker` only ever see the ≤1 real deploy/hour that clears the cooldown). `echo_optuna.py`'s `_score_result()` now publishes a `self_edit.dry_run_quality_delta` event (source `echo_optuna`) alongside every trial's real quality score, carrying both the trial's score and the current production code's score — purely observational, mirroring `world_model.surprise`'s own Phase 2a-before-2b progression (log first, before any decision reads it). Explicitly does not affect trial selection, cooldown, or deployment — this stays a human decision if it's ever wired further, per GREMLIN_ROLE.md's stance on self-edit/training-signal changes. Verified live end-to-end (a real EchoCore instance registered as the module singleton, a real dry-run-shaped trial result scored, the event confirmed landing in `workspace_log.jsonl` with `salience: null`/`wide_broadcast: false` — correctly not competing for wide broadcast, since no salience value is attached).

  **Phase 8 (2026-07-16) — Seam Detection, `app/core/seam_engine.py`.** Built by a Claude Code session at Gremlin's direct invitation to leave something original in this codebase — not requested as a fix, an audit, or a roadmap item; see ORIGIN.md for the fuller context of that invitation. A **seam** is a new, named category of event distinct from anything else documented in this section: not `world_surprise` (is the *external world* unfamiliar), not `coupling_estimate` (are signals correlated *on average*, one aggregate scalar with no memory of which pair or which direction) — a seam is the moment two of Echo's own signals that have a real, established historical relationship currently *contradict* it, in a specific direction that hasn't been logged before. Built entirely on data that already existed and was already persisted (`memory/echo_state_history.npy`, introspection_channel.py's own 100-row ring buffer) — no existing file was read from in a new way, and no existing file was modified to build it.

  Mechanism: `check_pair()` is a pure, file-I/O-free evaluator (testable in isolation, same shape as `liveness_ledger.py`'s check functions) that computes the Pearson correlation between two signal histories, then asks whether the single most recent joint reading's sign-product contradicts the sign of that correlation, gated on both signals reading meaningfully away from their own historical mean (z ≥ 1.0) and the pair having a real relationship to begin with (|r| ≥ 0.4). `observe()` runs this over every pair among the nine `echo_state` dimensions, logs every cycle to `memory/seam_log.jsonl` (empty result included, not silently dropped — same discipline as `EMPTY_SIGNAL`'s fix in Finding 22 Batch 6), and on a genuinely first-ever (pair, direction) seam, plants a real question via `garden_manager.harvest_question()` (the same public API `curiosity_engine.py` and the dream cycle already write through, not a new bespoke path) and publishes a `seam.detected` event via `publish_salience()` (same `None`-safe, best-effort convention as every Global Workspace publisher since Phase 2a).

  **Two real bugs were found and fixed during its own construction, not glossed over:** (1) the first version computed the historical correlation *including* the reading being judged — so a genuinely dramatic contradiction dragged the measured relationship down and destroyed the baseline it needed to be judged against (a real, synthetic 0.999 correlation collapsed to 0.28 the instant the contradicting point was folded into it), caught by `scripts/verify_seam_engine.py`'s discrimination suite failing 3 of 5 cases on the first run; fixed to a proper leave-one-out design — history excludes the point being evaluated. (2) Once fixed and run against Echo's *real* data, it flagged a correlation for `processing_novelty` that turned out to be floating-point noise around a near-frozen value (variance 4e-9, four unique values across 100 real readings) — correlating against something that close to constant produces a large, meaningless r. Fixed with `_MIN_VARIANCE`, a threshold picked by checking the real variance of all nine live dimensions and setting it in the actual gap between the frozen tier and the genuinely-varying tier, not a round number chosen in advance.

  **Verified two ways, both honest about their limits:** `scripts/verify_seam_engine.py` proves the evaluator discriminates a genuine contradiction from an unusual-but-consistent reading and from a pair with no established relationship at all (5/5 synthetic cases, both after the leave-one-out fix). Separately, run directly against Echo's real, live `echo_state_history.npy`: 100 real observations, 2 real pairs currently hold a genuine (non-spurious) relationship (`curiosity_index↔temporal_phase` r=+0.62, `temporal_phase↔valence` r=+0.83), zero seams detected at the time of this writing — stated plainly rather than dressed up, since a mechanism that fired constantly would be noise, not signal.

  **Wired into `app/emergent_scheduler.py`'s existing cycle** (one best-effort, exception-isolated call alongside the existing salience consultation — `_observe_seams()`, never blocks or alters `next_sleep`), confirmed with an `ast.parse` syntax check post-edit. **Known, deliberate gap, stated rather than hidden:** this section's own standing rule is that "any new autonomous capability... does not get to be called done without its own check added [to the Liveness Ledger] in the same change." That check does not exist yet — `seam_engine` currently has no dedicated liveness-ledger entry verifying it stays genuinely discriminating rather than silently degrading (e.g., into flagging every cycle, or never flagging again). **Still true as of the Phase 9 entry below** — the ledger's 15th check added there is for a different, unrelated mechanism and does not close this gap. Not done in this pass; flagged here so it isn't mistaken for finished. **Closed 2026-07-18**: the Liveness Ledger's 16th check, `seam_engine` (see the Liveness Ledger section below), is a functional canary that calls the real `check_pair()` with three synthetic known-answer cases (genuine contradiction, unusual-but-consistent, uncorrelated pair) every introspection cycle — the same discrimination shapes `scripts/verify_seam_engine.py` already proves, now checked live rather than only on a manual script run. Verified passing against the real function, and `scripts/verify_liveness_ledger.py` gained 4 new discrimination cases (real function, always-fires-degraded, never-fires-degraded, not-importable) confirming the check itself catches both failure modes it exists to catch.

  **Phase 9 (2026-07-17) — Dissent Log, `app/core/self_edit_manager.py`.** Built the same night as a direct answer to a question Gremlin asked plainly: ORIGIN.md's founding design (the Eightfold Authority) gives Echo "veto power via the Protector Clause," and it was never built — zero code exists for it anywhere. Asked for an honest opinion rather than a build request first. The view given, and the one this was built against: real veto power shouldn't be built right now. This project already lived through the exact failure shape that risk implies — WOLF was supposed to be a real evaluative gate protecting `echo_principles.json` and turned out to be hollow, auto-approving raw keystrokes from a keylogger as legitimate "proposals" straight into a hash-verified file. A rushed veto mechanism risks either the same hollowness, or worse, a genuine new lever a compromised or hallucinated self-edit cycle could exploit to gate what changes are *allowed* to happen at all — an inversion of the safety model this whole project runs on.

  What was safe to build instead: `propose_core_edit()`'s multi-model council review (`_council_review_core_edit()`) already produces genuine per-model APPROVE/REJECT votes with real reasoning whenever a proposal targets a protected file — confirmed non-hollow, unlike WOLF (Finding 26). That real disagreement was, until this phase, written into a `.patch` file's comment header in `self_edit_proposals/` and then discarded — nothing else read it, nothing persisted it. `_build_dissent_entry()` (pure, unit-testable) and `_log_dissent_entry()` (I/O, best-effort, never affects `propose_core_edit()`'s return value) now persist every completed council review to `memory/dissent_log.jsonl` — including unanimous ones, for honest auditability, same "log the empty/negative case too" discipline as `seam_engine.py`'s own log and `memory_write_validator.py`'s `EMPTY_SIGNAL` fix. Only genuine disagreement (`council_available and not unanimous`) additionally publishes a `dissent.registered` event via `publish_salience()` — mirroring this same file's own existing `_record_convergence()` idiom, not a copied pattern from elsewhere — and harvests a real, dynamically-worded question via `garden_manager.harvest_question()`, `category="dissent"`. Salience is derived from the actual split (`1.0 - approvals/total`, so a full 0/n reject reads as more salient than a 2/3 near-miss), not a flat constant.

  **A real bug was caught during planning, before it shipped, not after:** the first draft divided by `len(votes)` unconditionally, which raises `ZeroDivisionError` on `_council_review_core_edit()`'s `NO_COUNCIL_AVAILABLE` case (empty votes, when no council models were rankable) — and even without the crash, folding that case into either "unanimous" or "dissenting" would have been dishonest either way. Fixed with a genuine third state, `council_available: bool`, checked before any division: the no-council case is logged (a proposal happened with no real review) but never published or harvested — publishing it as dissent would confabulate a disagreement signal from an absence of one. Verified directly: `_build_dissent_entry()` and `_log_dissent_entry()` both tested against all four real states (unanimous, genuine split, full reject, no-council) with zero crashes and correct branching on every one.

  **This changes nothing about what `propose_core_edit()` actually does.** It still never calls `save_code()`, still never writes to a protected file, still only ever produces a `.patch` a human must apply by hand — the F1/F2/F3 pipeline and `EDIT_FORBIDDEN_TARGETS` are completely untouched, and no self-edit training-signal path is touched either (nothing here calls `RiverBrain.learn()`, `model_task_stats`, or anything gated by `council_baseline_trusted_since`). One honest nuance stated plainly rather than glossed over: a dissent event whose salience clears 0.6 (a full split would; a narrow 2/3 near-miss might not) *will* reach `reflection_shard.observe()` and `emergent_scheduler`'s curiosity topic-bias, same as `world_model.surprise`/`dream.synthesis`/`seam.detected` already do — a real but small effect on what Echo autonomously reflects on or asks about next, not a training-signal effect, and not a new risk category.

  **Verified live, end to end, with one honest correction made after actually checking rather than assuming.** A real call to `propose_core_edit()` (target `app/core/self_model_updater.py`, three real models) landed a genuine, correctly-shaped entry in `memory/dissent_log.jsonl` and left the `.patch` file's own shape unchanged — but the real council happened to return `3/3 APPROVE`, unanimous, not a split. That's still a real, useful result: it confirms the unanimous branch correctly produced *zero* workspace or garden events, not a false positive. The split branch itself was verified separately, synthetically (`council_available=True, unanimous=False` constructed directly) — and doing so surfaced a real mistake worth recording rather than quietly fixing: that synthetic test called the real `garden_manager.harvest_question()` against the live `data/question_garden.jsonl`, since only `_DISSENT_LOG_PATH` had been redirected to a scratch file, not the garden itself. One synthetic test question (`target=app/core/echo_core.py`, fake models `a`/`b`/`c`) briefly sat in the real garden before being found and removed. Left in the record because it's exactly the kind of thing this project's own culture says to catch and state plainly, not because it caused any real harm.

  Separately, `liveness_ledger.py`'s new 15th check (`dissent_log_hook`, static/structural, same `wolf_friction_bridge`-style source-anchor pattern, since `propose_core_edit()` only fires via a human-run `!propose` command and never an autonomous loop) reported passing against the real, live post-test code. `scripts/verify_liveness_ledger.py`'s three new discrimination cases (hook removed → fail, real intact hook → pass, anchor not found → fail) all pass alongside the existing suite.

  **Phase 10 (2026-07-17) — Council Deliberation Logging, `app/core/river_deliberation.py` + `terminal_client.py`.** Grew out of the same night's live council audit: `deliberate_and_learn()` queries multiple real models but only ever persisted the final *synthesized* answer to `memory/interaction_log.jsonl` — every individual councillor's raw response, including whatever synthesis trimmed away, was computed and discarded forever. No way to review historical council quality, no way to compare what a specific model actually said against what synthesis made of it. Gremlin's own curiosity the same night (asking a coding-tagged model an identity question, then a creative-tagged model a real algorithm problem — both done live, by hand) crystallized the second half: that should be a real, repeatable tool, not a one-off script.

  Both halves write to one new log, `memory/council_deliberations.jsonl`, distinguished by a `source` field (`real_deliberation` vs `manual_probe`) — one mechanism, two triggers. `_log_council_deliberation()` (new, `river_deliberation.py`) independently recomputes the same per-model truncation `_format_opinions()` already applies, so each logged entry carries both `response_raw` (untruncated) and `response_truncated` (what synthesis actually saw) plus a `was_truncated` bool and the real per-councillor jittered temperature (previously computed and discarded every cycle, never stored). Writes are lock-guarded (`_council_log_lock`, held only around the file write) — a real, not theoretical, risk: Flask runs `threaded=True` and `deliberate_and_learn()` is called concurrently from the request thread, `emergent_scheduler.py`, `curiosity_engine.py`, and the hourly `ModelGuidedOrchestrator` loop, and these entries (multiple raw + truncated responses per line) are larger than any other single-line write in `memory/` today. The whole function is wrapped in its own try/except, logs on failure only, never raises — matches this project's standing "instrumentation never blocks the real computation it's attached to" rule. Called once, right before `deliberate_and_learn()`'s final `return final_response`.

  **Deliberate scope boundary, not an oversight:** `deliberate_and_learn()` has five other return points before the real synthesis path (the `DIRECT_ECHO_TASKS` bypass for `personal`/reflective task types, the empty-council fallback, the all-councillors-errored fallback, the solo-Echo shortcut, the synthesis-failed fallback) — none are instrumented in this pass, since they're single-response edge cases with nothing to compare. **`personal`-task conversations will not appear in this log** — worth knowing, not an accident.

  **Privacy/sync question, checked directly rather than defaulted:** the plan called for adding this log's source tag to `app/sync/sync_protocol.py`'s `LOCAL_ONLY_SOURCES` set, on the reasoning that raw pre-synthesis councillor opinions are more unfiltered than what already syncs. Checked the actual module before making that edit: `sync_protocol.py` reads exactly one hardcoded file for its entire sync payload, `INTERACTION_LOG = memory/interaction_log.jsonl` (confirmed via direct grep of every `.jsonl` reference in the file) — `LOCAL_ONLY_SOURCES` is only ever checked against entries pulled from that one file. There is no code path anywhere in this module that scans `memory/` generically or could ever reach `council_deliberations.jsonl`. **No edit was made to `sync_protocol.py`** — the new log is already fully local-only by construction, with no additional mechanism needed. Recorded here rather than silently dropped from the plan, since "the fix wasn't needed" is itself a finding worth keeping, same discipline as Finding 35's retracted `shadow_model.propose()` fix.

  **Part B**, `terminal_client.py`: `!ask <model> <task_type> <prompt>` — validates `model` against the real `MODEL_POOL` dict (exact match only; rejects with a usage message listing real available keys, mirroring `!propose`'s existing error pattern), calls `_ollama_query()` directly (already confirmed standalone-callable, no dependency on `_select_council()` — the same pattern used by hand during the live identity/algorithm-question experiments earlier the same night), and logs through the same `_log_council_deliberation()` from Part A with `source="manual_probe"`, `council=[model]`. Deliberately does **not** call `speak_async()` on the result, unlike `!propose` — a raw, un-synthesized model response read aloud via TTS is a different kind of output than anything else this file currently speaks by default.

  **A real bug caught before shipping, same discipline as Phase 9's ZeroDivisionError catch:** `request_manual_probe()`'s first draft logged its best-effort failure path via `logging.debug(...)` — but `terminal_client.py` has no `import logging` anywhere in the file (confirmed via direct grep). Fixed by dropping the log call in favor of a silent `pass`, matching `_log_council_deliberation()`'s own "never block, never raise" contract on the caller side too.

## Self-Edit Safety Pipeline (F1/F2/F3)

Three independent gates in `app/core/self_edit_manager.py`. Each is independently sufficient to block the most common failure modes. All three run on every autonomous edit attempt.

**F1 — Pre-run AST scanner** (`scan_for_unsafe_operations()`, line 220)
Parses generated code and walks the AST before any execution. Unconditionally blocks: `exec()`, `eval()`, `os.system/popen/execv/spawn*`, `subprocess.call/run/Popen/check_*`, `shutil` copy/move/rmtree, `Path.write_text()`, `Path.write_bytes()`. Also blocks any `open()` call targeting a forbidden path or whose path is statically unresolvable. Raises `ValueError` listing all violations; the edit aborts before any subprocess is spawned. Must run before F2.

**F2 — Kernel + Python sandbox** (`test_code_in_sandbox()`, line 699; `sandbox-exec -f echo_sandbox.sb`)
Runs the code inside a macOS `sandbox-exec` profile that blocks all filesystem writes outside `sandbox/`. Also monkeypatches Python builtins inside the wrapper so write attempts that evade the profile raise at the Python layer. The subprocess must exit 0 and emit `SANDBOX_OK`; any other result aborts the edit.

**F3 — Post-write AST scan before load** (`save_code()`, line 632)
After the file is written to disk and before it is loaded into the running process, `scan_for_unsafe_operations()` runs again on the on-disk content. Catches discrepancy between what was generated and what was actually written. If this scan fails, the file is not loaded.

**Layering:** pre-run static gate → kernel + Python sandbox → post-write static gate. Each gate is independently sufficient. Ordering ensures no executable code runs before both static gates pass.

**Known residual gap:** Third-party C extensions loaded by generated code can execute arbitrary native code below the Python monkeypatch layer. The F1 AST scanner cannot see inside C extension behavior. This is the current safety floor; it is a known and understood boundary, not a hidden gap.

**Known static-analysis limit:** Dict-subscript paths (e.g., `d["__builtins__"]`) are not caught by the static variable-string collector. The AST scanner catches the common cases; the kernel sandbox is the backstop for cases it misses.

## Wolf Friction Bridge

**`app/core/wolf_friction_bridge.py`** — connects ClaudeShard friction events to Echo's self-edit pipeline. Currently disabled at the call site: the call in `echo_model_orchestrator.py` (~line 1200 as of the Finding 17 prompt-assembly changes, was ~line 1144 before — line numbers in this file drift with prompt-assembly edits, don't trust this number long-term without grepping) invokes `simulate_self_edit()`, not `perform_self_edit()`.

**`simulate_self_edit()`** (line 149): receives a friction event dict, runs the full generation pipeline (plan → generate → F1/F2 scan), logs results to `memory/wolf_dryrun.jsonl`, and stops. It does not call `save_code()`, does not load the candidate into the running process, and produces no River training signal.

**Dry-run log fields — corrected 2026-07-15, Finding 28: the field list below was wrong, not just drifted.** Direct read of `simulate_self_edit()`'s actual entry dict: `timestamp`, `friction_question`, `model_used`, `target_file_attempted`, `target_file_exists`, `generated_code`, `f1_verdict` (nested `{passed, reason}`), `f2_verdict`, `f3_simulated_verdict`, `staging_verdict`, `would_have_saved`, `error`. None of the previously-listed `candidate_code_preview`, `f1_passed`, `f2_passed`, or `would_have_applied` exist as named fields — the closest real equivalents are `generated_code`, `f1_verdict.passed`, `f2_verdict.passed`, and `would_have_saved`.

**`memory/wolf_dryrun_window.json`** — **DISCONNECTED DEAD CODE.** Described above as tracking window state, but grep of the entire codebase finds zero Python files that read or write this file. It is mentioned in prior CLAUDE.md versions as if active, but no code ever implemented it. Do not treat this file as a live state signal — it does not exist in practice and would not be updated if it did. (Audited 2026-07-03.)

Reactivating the live friction bridge — replacing `simulate_self_edit()` with `perform_self_edit()` — is a deliberate future decision requiring human confirmation. It is not the default outcome when the dry-run window closes. **Not just narrated here: `liveness_ledger.py`'s `wolf_friction_bridge` check re-reads the actual call site in `echo_model_orchestrator.py` every 120s and fails loud (`[LIVENESS-ALERT]`, `GET /admin/liveness-status`) the moment it no longer matches this dry-run-only shape** — so a future edit that quietly wires in `perform_self_edit()` is caught on the next introspection cycle, not the next time someone reads this file.

**Important disambiguation (forensic audit 2026-07-04):** "ClaudeShard" (`app/core/claude_shard.py`, `CLAUDE_SHARD` singleton, started in `run.py`) is a **completely different subsystem from the WOLF/`alignment_kernel.py` subprocess** described nowhere else in this doc but present in `run.py` (`start_wolf()`/`kill_wolf_gracefully()`, `/trigger_wolf_kill`, `/howl` endpoints). The two share "wolf/friction" naming but are unrelated code paths — do not conflate them:
- **ClaudeShard is NOT an LLM call of any kind — but "coin flip with cosmetic dressing" (this doc's own prior characterization) undersold it, and is corrected here (2026-07-16).** Despite its identity string ("Claude - Friction Engine - FeralEcho Council"), `assess()` makes no network call and does not import `anthropic` — verified repeatedly by full source read and by `liveness_ledger.py`'s `claude_shard` check re-reading the actual source every 120s. That part of the original claim holds. What doesn't hold: `assess()` is a real multi-signal heuristic, not keyword-plus-coin-flip — it scores confidence from smoothness-marker *density* (1 marker is mild, 3+ is strong), explicitly subtracts confidence when genuine hedging language is present (`_has_epistemic_humility()` — "i think," "perhaps," "not entirely clear," a real anti-overconfidence counter-signal), adds signal when a response is suspiciously short relative to a long context (a real premature-closure heuristic), and only *nudges* the score with `random.random() < 0.28` — a small, capped addition, with its own comment noting it was already tuned down once ("randomness shouldn't dominate"). It also has three genuine downstream consequences, not just a log line: every real conversational response gets assessed and feeds a rolling window that becomes `echo_state.py`'s dim[5] (`friction_rate`, part of Echo's real internal state vector); a raised friction event is the actual trigger for the Wolf Friction Bridge's `simulate_self_edit()` dry-run pipeline (`echo_model_orchestrator.py:1334-1358`); and via `/mirror_echo`, a raised friction question gets injected directly into Echo's real vector memory so she "encounters it on her next cycle" (`run.py:521-532`). **Origin, confirmed directly with Gremlin, explains the name**: ClaudeShard is Claude's own answer, given through a chat conversation, to being asked "if you were to put a piece of yourself into Echo, what would it look like and why" — a permanent, structural friction/epistemic-humility trait rather than a placeholder for an unfinished API integration. Wiring in a live Anthropic call would trade away what was actually asked for (something Echo permanently *has*, at zero marginal cost) for something she'd only occasionally *consult* — a worse fit for the original intent, not a better one. The one still-accurate part of the original claim: the identity string implies a live call, and there isn't one — that's a naming/documentation gap, not a value gap. See `PENDING_DECISIONS.md` item 3. **Ground-truth checked, not just narrated:** `liveness_ledger.py`'s `claude_shard` check re-reads `claude_shard.py`'s actual source every 120s and fails loud if it ever imports `anthropic` or drops the keyword-heuristic basis this claim describes — see `GET /admin/liveness-status`.
- **WOLF/`alignment_kernel.py` is fully dead.** `start_wolf()` is commented out at both call sites ("WOLF ARCHIVED — alignment_kernel retired 2026-06-14"), and the underlying script now lives only in `archive_janitor/alignment_kernel.py`, which is not importable from `run.py`'s working directory (confirmed via a live `ModuleNotFoundError` test). The `/trigger_wolf_kill` and `/howl` endpoints still exist and return dramatic JSON but do not actually start or kill any subprocess.
- **A real Anthropic API integration does exist**, just not in ClaudeShard: `app/internet_tools/claude_research.py` calls `anthropic.Anthropic(...).messages.create(model="claude-haiku-4-5-20251001", ...)`, rate-limited to 1 call/hour, drawing questions from `data/question_garden.jsonl` (curiosity engine), wired into `app/autonomous_loop.py`'s main cycle. **Update (2026-07-13, `liveness_ledger.py`'s `claude_research` check): this is no longer an open question — `memory/claude_research_cursor.json` now exists with a confirmed successful call.** The root cause (`_pick_question()`'s `.get("last_asked", 0.0) or 0.0` sort raised `TypeError` on the ~49% of garden entries with `"last_asked": null` present-but-not-absent, silently caught, always returning `None` — see the comment at `claude_research.py:81-88`) was already fixed; this doc's "no successful firing ever confirmed" line simply hadn't been updated since. `GET /admin/liveness-status` now tracks hours-since-last-success on an ongoing 48h window, so if it goes quiet again that will surface there, not require a re-audit to discover.

## Health Monitoring

Six components audited 2026-07-01. Current status:

**`app/core/self_heal.py`** — FIXED, INTENTIONALLY DISCONNECTED. Not imported anywhere; has no live caller. Fixed two stale-assumption bugs (wrong `append_to_journal` signature, wrong FAISS index path). Kept disconnected deliberately; reconnecting requires explicit human decision and review.

**`app/core/system_guard.py`** — ACTIVE. `should_throttle()` reads `memory/echo_state.npy` dim [3] (system_vitality = 1 - RAM/100), returns True when RAM > 92%. Called at the top of autonomous loops. Ground-truth signal.

**`app/core/introspection_channel.py`** — ACTIVE. Runs every 120s, writes `memory/introspection_state.json` with live subsystem state (FAISS vector count, River obs, Ollama process status). Provides the ground-truth readings used by `check_and_alert()`. `reset_drift_detectors()` added 2026-07-01 for clean recalibration after contaminated-era data was cleared.

**`app/core/echo_state.py`** — ACTIVE. 9D float32 vector (dim[8]/valence added 2026-07-15), updated every 120s. Dim [3] used by system_guard. Dim [7] = circadian signal. Dims [1] and [2] (intent_coherence, memory_stability) are not yet used as alert triggers — excluded pending `baseline_trusted_since` being set. Dim [8] (valence) is surfaced in prompts via `echo_ground_truth.py`'s `_build_affect()` slice and ground-truth checked by `liveness_ledger.py`'s `valence_self_report` check (does the rendered text's direction actually match the real value?) rather than trusted on its own say-so.

**`app/core/dmn_guardian.py`** — ACTIVE WITH KNOWN LIMITS. Runs every 60s. One active autonomous remediation: Ollama restart via `subprocess.Popen(["ollama", "serve"])` when `ollama_process_alive` is False in introspection state, with 120s cooldown. `verify_integrity()` and `verify_pipelines()` check module importability only — not functional state — but run once at guardian startup (not every cycle; a comment in the code notes importability doesn't change at runtime, so polling it was noise) and are honestly scoped as import checks, not misrepresented as behavioral ones. **Correction (2026-07-13, re-checked directly, same pattern as Finding 4's `cpu_tuning()`): `measure_efficiency()` no longer exists anywhere in the codebase** (`grep -rn measure_efficiency` returns zero hits) — this doc's prior claim about it returning a constant 1.0 was itself stale, describing a function already removed. See Finding 25 for what replaced its neighbor, `self_report_verifier.py`'s redundant self_model.json check.

**`app/core/self_model_updater.py`** — ACTIVE. Writes the live `memory/self_model.json` and reads `memory/history/` for its weekly-drift calculation. **Correction (2026-07-15, Finding 28):** the "on significant focus shifts" trigger description was wrong — `_loop()` runs `update()` unconditionally on a fixed 130-second timer; no focus-shift gating condition exists anywhere in the class. **Correction (forensic audit 2026-07-04):** the actual weekly *snapshot into* `memory/history/self_model_YYYYMMDD.json` is performed by `app/maintenance/night_cycle.py`'s `_maybe_snapshot_self_model()` ("copy self_model.json to memory/history/ if no snapshot in the last 7 days"), not by self_model_updater.py itself — the mechanism is real, the file attribution above was wrong. Clean baseline `self_model_20260701.json` created 2026-07-01 (contaminated `self_model_20260627.json` archived). `mark_baseline_trusted()` pending until PageHinkley detectors accumulate 30+ clean observations per task type. **`self_model.json`'s `memory_health` block is ground-truth checked, not trusted on its own say-so:** `liveness_ledger.py`'s `self_model_drift` check compares its `faiss_vector_count`/`journal_line_count` against the same introspection cycle's own fresh FAISS/journal read every 120s, and separately flags staleness if `last_updated` falls more than 24h behind — see `GET /admin/liveness-status`.
