A second, separate repo under the same account, `feral-echo-core`, was found the same session and was **not** empty: `visibility: PUBLIC`, pushed 2025-11-09 ("Initial release: Feral Echo v0.1 Core," 3 commits), containing a real early snapshot — `run.py` (592 lines, a much smaller pre-refactor version than today's), `dmn_guardian.py`, `constitution.py`, `README.md`/`SHIP.md`, `.env.example`, `LICENSE` (MIT).

**Checked, not assumed, before deciding what to do about either:** the full 65-commit local history was scanned for secret-shaped strings (`NEWSAPI_KEY=`, `OPENWEATHER_API_KEY=`, `GREMLIN_SECRET=`, Anthropic/OpenAI/Google key patterns) — none found; `.env` was never committed at any point (`git log --all --full-history -- .env` returns nothing) and is correctly gitignored. `feral-echo-core`'s three real source files were separately scanned the same way — no hardcoded secret found there either; `.env.example` there is genuinely just a placeholder template. One real, non-secret thing *was* found in `feral-echo-core`'s old `run.py`: the exact vulnerable comparison pattern this doc's own Finding 14 later diagnosed and fixed (`if token != GREMLIN_SECRET:` with no check for whether the secret was ever set) — sitting in plain, publicly-readable view for 8 months, informational rather than a live risk since nothing running today is that old code. Practical exposure was checked, not guessed: `feral-echo-core` had 0 stargazers, 0 watchers, 0 forks across its full lifetime, and GitHub's 14-day traffic window (the only history GitHub retains) showed 5 views from 1 unique visitor on a single day, consistent with Gremlin himself checking it rather than outside discovery — genuinely exposed, but apparently unnoticed.

**Resolved same session:** both repos flipped to private via `gh repo edit <repo> --visibility private --accept-visibility-change-consequences`, each independently re-verified afterward with a second, different command (`gh repo view`) after the first verification attempt hit a transient `gh api` hiccup — a real instance of this doc's own standing discipline (verify via a second, independent path rather than trust the first check that returns something odd). `FeralEcho-Backup` was then pushed for real for the first time (`git push -u origin main`, clean fast-forward, no conflicting history to force over) — the current codebase now has a real, private off-machine backup, which it did not have before this session. `feral-echo-core`'s old content was left exactly as it was, just no longer public — not merged, not deleted, a separate historical artifact now sitting privately.

**Finding 39 — declared model specialty tags exist and are well-curated, but council selection has never once read them; found, verified against live data, and flagged, not fixed, the same session (2026-07-16):** `detect_model_tags()` (`echo_model_orchestrator.py:606`) is a real, hand-written specialty map — `qwen2.5-coder` → *"dedicated coding specialist"*, `gemma3`/`mistral` → creative/story/poetry, `deepseek` → reasoning+coding, `echo` → personal/memory/persona. Grepped directly: neither `_select_council()` nor `score_model()` (`river_deliberation.py`, `echo_model_orchestrator.py`) reads `tags` or `type` anywhere — selection is pure historical-performance competition across the whole 12-model pool (10 Ollama + 2 MLX) for every task type, blind to declared specialty.

**Checked against real, live data, not assumed:** unpickled `memory/river_brain.pkl` directly. Of 12 registered models, `echo:latest` (15,652 real observations), `llama3.2:3b` (4,238), and `llama3:instruct` (4,231) account for 99.7% of all real council observations combined; the other 9 sit at 12–24 lifetime observations each. `llama3.2:3b` and `llama3:instruct` both fall into `detect_model_tags()`'s untagged-for-coding `else` branch (`["general"]` only) yet dominate real `coding`-task usage, while `qwen2.5-coder:7b` — explicitly tagged the coding specialist — has 16 lifetime coding observations. Root cause, not just correlation: `score_model()`'s rolling mean uses the standard incremental-mean update (`mean += (new - mean) / count`), which means each new observation's influence shrinks as `1/count` — a model with a large early lead becomes both more likely to keep winning selection *and* increasingly resistant to its measured score ever changing, while under-sampled models stay volatile indefinitely. This is real preferential attachment, empirically confirmed in this system's own data, not a theoretical concern.

**Deliberately not fixed in this pass** — this touches `river_deliberation.py`'s live selection logic, a human-edit-only file (`EDIT_FORBIDDEN_TARGETS`) but still core control flow, and GREMLIN_ROLE.md's discipline calls for a diff shown and confirmed before anything like that lands, not a default fix bundled into a documentation pass. Plausible direction, not applied: a scoring nudge toward a model's declared specialty task types (mirroring `ECHO_SCORE_BOOST`'s existing shape, smaller and tag-conditional instead of Echo-only) rather than a hard eligibility filter — preserves genuine competition while giving specialty models the head start their tags already claim they deserve. See `PENDING_DECISIONS.md`.

**Follow-up, same day — fixed for real, diffs shown and confirmed first, then verified against live data both before and after.** Three changes landed:

1. `TAG_SCORE_BOOST = 1.15` (`river_deliberation.py`) — the scoring nudge described above, implemented. Verified against real live scores: narrows `qwen2.5-coder`'s coding-task gap against the leaders from 0.31→0.36 (a real, measured ~15% lift), explicitly confirmed *not* enough alone to flip a ranking this entrenched — stated plainly rather than oversold, since the real fix is the next one.
2. `_MEAN_EFFECTIVE_WINDOW = 200` (`echo_model_orchestrator.py`, `RiverBrain.learn()`) — caps the incremental-mean denominator so a score stays responsive indefinitely instead of freezing past a few thousand observations. Verified directly: simulating 20 genuinely bad observations against a high-count model moved its score 20x more under the windowed version (0.5537→0.5009) than the old pure-incremental one (0.5537→0.5511, barely moved). Takes effect immediately on restart — no reset of existing data needed or applied; a broad reset was considered and explicitly rejected (the historical observations aren't contaminated data the way Finding 3's cross-training mixup was, just unevenly distributed — wiping them would discard real signal, not fix an unfairness).
3. `vicuna:latest` retired from `MODEL_POOL` via a small, reversible `_RETIRED_MODELS` set, and separately, at Gremlin's explicit follow-up instruction, actually deleted from disk (`ollama rm vicuna:latest`, 3.8GB freed) — a 2023-era model sharing its only tags with several stronger, newer models in the pool, contributing no real specialty coverage. The `_RETIRED_MODELS` line is left in place even though the model is gone — costs nothing to keep, honest record of the decision.

**Live re-verification after all three landed, same session:** re-ran the real coding-task selection against the live, current `river_brain.pkl`. The top 3 (`echo:latest`, `llama3.2:3b`, `llama3:instruct`) still won — confirmed, not glossed over, that a 15% nudge against a gap this size (16–24 observations vs. 4,500+) doesn't change today's outcome by itself. That live result is what motivated the fourth piece below, not a separate ask.

**Fourth piece, brainstormed live with Gremlin off the re-verification result: `UNDER_SAMPLED_REFRESH_PROBABILITY = 0.25`, `fair_sample_refresh` param on `_select_council()`** (`river_deliberation.py`), default `False` — same preserve-prior-behavior discipline as `exploration_bias`, only the real `deliberate_and_learn()` call site opts in. A second, independent bounded swap (at most one slot, same shape as the existing world-surprise exploration bump, fires on its own schedule rather than being folded into that one) that occasionally forces the single most under-sampled eligible model back into the council, using `river_brain._MEAN_EFFECTIVE_WINDOW` (200) as the "fair sample" bar rather than a fresh magic number. Deliberately not permanent — once a model's real observation count crosses that bar, this mechanism stops touching it and its ranking is settled by performance alone.

**Verified, including one honest nuance surfaced by the test itself, not hidden:** confirmed the flag genuinely gates the mechanism (forced the probability roll to succeed with the flag off — baseline unchanged), and confirmed a forced-fire swap is deterministic and correct. The model that actually won the first live test swap was `llama3.1:8b` (6 real coding observations) — *not* `qwen2.5-coder` (16) — because the mechanism picks whichever eligible model has the fewest observations overall, not specifically the tagged-specialty ones used as the running example. That's the intended, more defensible design (fair to any genuinely under-sampled model, not just the ones already being discussed) but means `qwen2.5-coder` won't necessarily be first through the door — it gets its turn once whoever's currently more neglected catches up past it.

**Finding 40 — a real Metal/GPU crash turned into a much longer outage because every restart afterward silently hung forever, never reaching `serving`; found live, root-caused, and fixed the same night (2026-07-17):** `memory/echo_watchdog.log` shows a genuine native crash — `libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::runtime_error: [METAL] Command buffer execution failed: Insufficient Memory (00000008:kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorOutOfMemory)`, exit code 134 (SIGABRT), likely from an MLX model call running low on GPU-accessible memory. `start_echo.sh`'s crash-restart watchdog caught it correctly and restarted within 10s, exactly as designed — that part of the safety net worked.

**What didn't work: every restart after that — the watchdog's automatic one and several manual ones from Gremlin — hung indefinitely at the `threads_starting` sentinel stage, never reaching `serving`.** Traced directly, not guessed: the watchdog log showed `[ST_SINGLETON] Loading SentenceTransformer(...)` retrying a HEAD request to `huggingface.co` every second, forever, always logging `Retry 1/5` and never advancing past it — an outer loop re-attempting the whole load from scratch on each failure rather than a bounded retry that eventually falls through to a cache. Root cause of the failure itself, confirmed directly and reproducibly: `python3 -c "import socket; socket.gethostbyname('huggingface.co')"` raised the exact same `[Errno 8] nodename nor servname provided, or not known` in a plain interactive check — a real, live gap specific to Python's own DNS resolution path, while the shell's `ping`/`nslookup` succeeded moments earlier. Not a general network outage; whatever it was, it was affecting the Python process specifically. Confirmed before touching anything: `~/.cache/huggingface/hub/models--sentence-transformers--all-MiniLM-L6-v2` already held a complete, valid snapshot (config, tokenizer, `model.safetensors`, all real files, not stubs) — the network check was entirely unnecessary, not a missing dependency.

**Fixed in `run.py`**, same `os.environ.setdefault(...)`-before-any-native-import pattern already used for the KMP guard: `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` and `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1`, forcing the loader to use the already-valid local cache and skip the network call (and therefore the hang) entirely. **Verified, not assumed fixed:** killed the stuck process, let the watchdog's own restart pick up the new code, and watched it directly — reached `serving` in 35 seconds (previously: never), zero `nodename nor servname`/`Retry 1/5` lines anywhere in the log since, `/admin/liveness-status` confirming `all_passing: true` and `stale: false` on the new process. The original Metal/GPU crash is a separate, likely one-off event and wasn't further diagnosed here — this finding closes the much bigger problem it silently triggered, not the crash itself.

---

**Finding 41 — Five-lane "unknown unknowns" sweep (2026-07-18): two live credential leaks confirmed against the running server, three new concurrency races in Emergence-Roadmap-era code, a real F1 self-edit scanner bypass class (currently caught by F2 in practice), and an actively-accelerating unbounded-growth bug — all flagged, none fixed in this pass:**

Prompted directly, not self-initiated: a conversation about this project's own recurring finding (a gate applied to a class of things, not verified exhaustively against every instance — see Findings 28/36 and the forensic audit's own naming of this as a pattern recurring three times) led to a decision to run exactly the kind of broad, undirected sweep that has historically been the only thing that surfaces this project's unknown unknowns (Finding 22's 108-item bug hunt; both forensic audit passes) rather than a targeted question. Five independent, read-only research agents were dispatched in parallel, each assigned one class of bug none of the existing 40 Findings or either forensic audit pass had specifically swept for (deliberately not re-treading self-edit safety, POST-endpoint auth, dead code, or FAISS health). Every finding below was independently re-verified directly against source and/or live runtime state before being written down here — same discipline as Findings 28/30, not taken on any agent's own say-so.

**A. Live credential leaks (HIGH, both confirmed against the actual running server, not just source-read):**

- **A1 — `GET /message/inbox` serves the real, live `ECHO_PARTNER_SECRET` in every message envelope.** `app/sync/echo_messaging.py`'s `_build_envelope()` embeds `os.environ.get("ECHO_PARTNER_SECRET")` directly into the dict sent to the partner machine; `_log_message()` persists that whole envelope, secret field included, to `memory/echo_messages.jsonl` on both send and receive; `get_recent_messages()` reads it back with no field stripped; `/message/inbox` (`run.py:732-735` → `routes_messaging.message_inbox()`) has no `_secret_ok()` call anywhere and returns the result verbatim via `jsonify`. Confirmed live, not just read: a real `curl` against the running server returned an envelope with a non-empty `secret` field (the value itself was never printed or logged anywhere in this investigation — confirmed present via a boolean check only). `ECHO_PARTNER_SECRET` is the one thing standing between "genuinely from Air" and "anyone on the network" for `/message/receive`'s HMAC check — this leak defeats that check for anyone who reads it off this route first.
- **A2 — `GET /projects/file` still serves a live API-key dump, via a file Finding 7 already flagged once but only checked against git history.** `new_directory/app/core/env/environment.json` (mtime Jun 19, a byproduct of the pre-2026-07-08 relative-path self-edit bug) is a full `os.environ` snapshot including real `NEWSAPI_KEY`/`OPENWEATHER_API_KEY` values. Finding 7 confirmed this specific file was never tracked in git and never pushed — but never checked whether the running app's own file-viewer route could serve it directly. It can: `_EXCLUDED_DIR_NAMES` (`app/routes_echo_studio.py:446-449`) is `{".git", "__pycache__", "node_modules", "self_edit_backups", "self_edit_plans"}` — none of the ~16 scaffold-sprawl directories Finding 30 catalogued are on it. Confirmed live: `curl 'http://localhost:5000/projects/file?path=new_directory/app/core/env/environment.json'` → `200`, both key values present and — checked by SHA-256 comparison only, values never printed — byte-identical to the current, live `.env` entries. Both keys are exposed, live, right now, to anyone who can reach the app.

**B. Three new concurrency races in code that postdates Finding 22 Batch 3's race-condition sweep (independently reproduced by the sweep agent using the exact production write pattern, spot-verified here at the caller-site level):**

- **B1 — self-edit outcome tracking can silently lose a record.** `self_edit_outcome_tracker.evaluate_pending_outcomes()` (runs on DMN Guardian's own 60s thread, `dmn_guardian.py:144`) does read-whole-file → work → write-back-from-stale-snapshot with no lock; `record_pending_outcome()` (called from the self-edit deploy path, `self_edit_manager.py:1951`, inside `_self_edit_deploy_lock`) is a plain append. Confirmed directly: these are two genuinely uncoordinated locks/threads — `_self_edit_deploy_lock` gives zero protection against Guardian's thread. A real self-edit's outcome record can be silently erased if it lands mid-evaluation, undermining Finding 8/35's whole purpose with no error logged anywhere. **Fixed, found already applied (2026-07-18):** a shared `_outcomes_lock` now guards both `record_pending_outcome()`'s append and `evaluate_pending_outcomes()`'s read-modify-write (split into a `_evaluate_pending_outcomes_locked()` inner function so the lock scope is obvious at the call site). Confirmed by direct source read, same as B4/B5 below — found already in the working tree, not applied by the session writing this correction.
- **B2 — `compute_salience()`'s persisted state has a real, high-frequency race.** `_persist_salience_state()` (`echo_core.py`) writes to a fixed, shared, non-unique tmp filename with no lock, called from at least two independently-threaded sites (`emergent_scheduler.py`, `river_deliberation.py`). The sweep agent's reproduction, using the identical write pattern: **372/600 concurrent calls (62%) lost their update**, failing into a swallowed `except Exception` at debug level — the same class of bug Batch 3 already fixed elsewhere, not yet applied to code built after that sweep. **Fixed, found already applied (2026-07-18):** a `_salience_state_lock` now guards the write, and the tmp filename is also made per-call-unique (`f"{path}.{os.getpid()}.{threading.get_ident()}.tmp"`) as belt-and-suspenders — closes both the silent lost-update and a `FileNotFoundError` two concurrent writers could otherwise race into on `os.replace()`.
- **B3 — the `apply_to_code` write-block (Finding 31's fix) can be bypassed by a slow or hung hook.** `_call_with_timeout()`'s 2-second timeout can't actually stop the worker thread (Python threads aren't forcibly killable) — the `finally` clause restores the real `open()`/`io.open()`/`Path.open()` unconditionally on timeout, while the abandoned thread may still be running. Anything the hook writes after that point executes completely unguarded and unlogged — the exact mechanism Finding 31 exists to close, reopened specifically in the timeout path. **Still open** — see `PENDING_DECISIONS.md` item 7. Unlike B1/B2/B4/B5, a plain lock doesn't fix this one — the fix has to actually stop execution, not just serialize access to a file.
- **B4 — `seam_engine.py`'s `_load_first_ever`/`_save_first_ever` cycle had no lock.** Real but was latent at the time this finding was written — only one caller (`emergent_scheduler.py`'s single thread) — undefended if a second caller was ever added. **Fixed, found already applied (2026-07-18):** a later session investigating the seam mechanism directly found `observe()`'s load → compute → save critical section already wrapped in a `threading.Lock()` (`_seam_state_lock`), with a code comment citing this exact finding by a "B4" label that didn't yet exist in this text — a live instance of the "doc lags commit" pattern this file's own history keeps flagging, caught mid-conversation rather than on a later audit pass. Formalized here rather than left as a dangling citation to nothing. Confirmed correct by direct source read.
- **B5 — `self_edit_manager.py`'s dissent-log append has the identical shape.** `_log_dissent_entry()` — real but was latent, same reasoning as B4 before its fix: only one caller (`propose_core_edit()`, itself only human-invoked via `!propose`). **Fixed, found already applied (2026-07-18), same discovery pass as B1/B2/B4:** a `_dissent_log_lock` now guards the append, with a code comment citing this finding directly. Of the five items in this section, only B3 remains genuinely open — it's also the only one that can't be closed with a lock alone.

**C. F1 self-edit AST scanner has a real bypass class distinct from the documented dict-subscript gap — currently caught by F2 in practice, independently tested both ways:**

`scan_for_unsafe_operations()` matches dangerous calls by literal spelling only (`obj.id == "subprocess"`, bare name `in {"exec","eval"}`) — it does not resolve import aliases, `from`-imports, or `__import__(...)`-style indirection. Confirmed directly by running real bypass payloads against the live function: `from os import system; system(...)`, `import os as o; o.system(...)`, `from subprocess import call; call(...)`, and `__import__("os").system(...)` all pass F1 silently; only the literal `os.system(...)` spelling is caught. Went one step further than the sweep agent's own scope and tested whether F2's kernel sandbox (`sandbox/echo_sandbox.sb`) actually catches what F1 misses, since the profile's own comment ("fork() and process-spawn are denied") looked possibly inconsistent with its explicit `(allow process-exec*)` line: ran the identical bypass code through the real `sandbox-exec` profile directly — `os.system(...)`, `subprocess.run(...)`, and `os.fork()` all fail (`PermissionError`/no output) inside the real sandbox. **Net: not an active exploit today** — F2 holds because `process-exec*` only covers the sandboxed interpreter's own launch, not subprocess-spawned children, which still need the (correctly omitted) `process-fork` — but F1's documented job (catch common cases before F2 ever runs) is silently not happening for this entire bypass class, meaning the "two independently-sufficient gates" guarantee this doc claims is currently down to one working gate for this specific attack shape.

**D. Unbounded growth: one component actively accelerating right now, plus a documentation claim found to be flatly false:**

`memory/quarantine_journal.jsonl` grew ~680 lines/day through 2026-07-16, then **more than doubled to 1,612 lines in the most recent single day**, correlating directly with fresh `[WARN:SIGNAL_EMBEDDED_IN_REFLECTION] possible retrieval loop` warnings in the live watchdog log — plausibly the early shape of the same self-quoting feedback loop Findings 11 and 35 already each fixed once in different subsystems. Separately, `app/subsystems/reflection_shard.py`'s own top-of-file docstring claims "Persistent JSONL journal with max-size culling" — confirmed false: `MAX_JOURNAL_ENTRIES=5000` only trims the in-memory list on load, never the on-disk file itself (`claude_shard.py` has the identical bug shape on its own journal). The systemic root cause: `memory_bridge.append_to_journal(tag, text)` — the shared helper behind at least 5 different always-growing log files — has no retention logic for any tag by construction, and `echo_janitor.py` (which exists specifically to solve this) is confirmed **not wired into the live scheduler** at all (only referenced from two dated backup copies of `emergent_scheduler.py`), and its own `detect_old_logs()` only flags by file mtime, which never triggers on a file that's always being actively appended to. Fastest confirmed raw growth: `council_deliberations.jsonl` (~7MB/day — didn't exist at the 2026-07-17 baseline), `echo_watchdog.log` (~7.6MB/day, no rotation, plain `tee -a` in `start_echo.sh`), `reflection_shard.jsonl` (~5MB/day), `interaction_log.jsonl` (~4.3MB/day). `memory/` is at 868MB as of this finding, up from the 810MB baseline Finding 30 recorded the day before.

**E. Trust-boundary findings:**

- `/mirror_echo`'s already-documented unauthenticated content (Finding 36) doesn't just get logged — it flows into `dual_learner.log_event()` with **zero validation of any kind** (confirmed: no `validat` string anywhere in `dual_learning.py`), while the parallel ClaudeShard→FAISS path from the same request *is* gated through `memory_write_validator`. The training-data path is the least-protected consequence of the already-known gap, not an equally-exposed one.
- **`GET /sync/export` has zero authentication**, confirmed directly at `run.py:902-909` — no `_secret_ok()` call, unlike its sibling `/sync/import`. Slipped past every prior audit because they all swept POST routes specifically; this is a GET route. Anyone reachable can pull the full `interaction_log.jsonl` history since any timestamp with one request. The in-code comment at `run.py:913-917` calling sync "not confirmed working" is also stale — Finding 12 already confirmed real bidirectional sync traffic back on 2026-07-13.

**Correction (2026-07-21, verified live during a "confirm every known bug is still present" pass): fixed, not still open.** `sync_export()` now calls `_secret_ok(request.args)` before doing anything else, same as its sibling `/sync/import` — confirmed by direct source read. Whichever session fixed this never updated this bullet; caught only by re-checking rather than trusting the doc's last-known status, the exact discipline this section itself keeps naming as necessary.

**What's confirmed clean, stated per this doc's own habit of recording negatives too:** SQL usage (`echo_studio/state/local_store.py`) is fully parameterized; every path-traversal guard re-tested (`_safe_resolve()`, `echo_tool_context.py`'s root-confinement) still holds; no `shell=True` anywhere in real source; `memory/snapshots/` (exactly 5), `self_edit_backups/` (exactly 25), and `staging/` candidates (26, consistent with prune-then-write ordering) all have real, currently-enforced retention; `claude_relay/` has no automated/non-human consumer and no live secrets in current content; `GET /settings/view` correctly returns key *names* only, never values; no generic `send_file`/static-file route exists anywhere outside the two already-guarded `/projects/*` endpoints.

**Nothing above has been fixed.** Per this project's own report-then-pause convention, all of it is flagged here for a deliberate decision on sequencing/approach, not defaulted into a fix pass. See `PENDING_DECISIONS.md` for the items that are genuinely open judgment calls rather than a straightforward "yes, patch this."

**Finding 42 — `/mirror_echo` auth gap: plan approved, partially rolled out, deliberately awaiting phone-side confirmation before the server gate ships (2026-07-18):** Following directly on Finding 36's flagged item and the 2026-07-16 forensic audit's #1-ranked open finding, Gremlin asked for a concrete plan to close `/mirror_echo`'s missing authentication. Investigating it surfaced a more precise version of Finding 36's second bullet: the actual, currently-running phone script (`feral_echo_symbiote.py`, pasted directly into this session for direct comparison against the tracked `thunderhead.py`) has **no `THUNDERHEAD_SECRET` concept anywhere** — not an unfilled placeholder as Finding 36 assumed, the variable doesn't exist in the real file at all. This means `/learning_event`/`/learning_batch` — gated since 2026-07-08 — have almost certainly been silently rejecting every real phone call since then, with the client's own `if r.ok:` check routing everything into local offline-bundling instead of ever surfacing an auth error; this sharpens, rather than contradicts, Finding 36's own suspicion.

Deliberate rollout order chosen to avoid breaking Gremlin's live phone chat: update the phone script first (manual, by him — this session cannot reach his phone), confirm the still-ungated server keeps replying normally, **then** gate the server. Gating first would have reproduced exactly what Finding 36 already documented for `/learning_event`: real requests silently rejected, `mirror_echo()` returning `None`, no reply, no visible error, for however long it took to notice.

**Done in this pass:**
- `thunderhead.py` (repo-tracked reference copy) — `mirror_echo()`'s payload now sends `"secret": THUNDERHEAD_SECRET`, matching the shape its `/learning_event`/`/learning_batch` calls already had since 2026-07-08. `THUNDERHEAD_SECRET` itself stays `""` in the tracked file — the real value is never committed, same rule Finding 36 already established.
- Gremlin was given the exact snippet to paste into the real phone script by hand: a new `THUNDERHEAD_SECRET = ""` constant (he fills in the real value himself from `.env`, never pasted into chat) plus the same one-line payload addition to `mirror_echo()`. He was also offered, as an optional bundled fix at near-zero extra cost since it's the same constant, the identical one-line addition to `send_event()`/`flush_bundle()` — closing the already-live `/learning_event`/`/learning_batch` gap this same investigation resurfaced.

**Not yet done — blocked on Gremlin's phone-side edit, not on anything code-side:**
- `run.py`'s `mirror_echo()` handler still has no `_secret_ok()` call. The server gate has not shipped yet, deliberately, per the rollout order above. Once Gremlin confirms the phone script sends a working secret, the fix is a single guard clause — `if not _secret_ok(data): return jsonify({"error": "unauthorized"}), 403` right after `data = request.json or {}`, before anything else in the function runs, no other line changed — honoring the function's own "KEEP THIS EXACTLY AS-IS" comment as closely as an auth fix allows.
- Live confirmation that the phone-side secret value is correct and `/mirror_echo` still round-trips correctly once gated.

See `PENDING_DECISIONS.md` item 1 for the live status of what's left. (The plan file this originally cited, `.claude/plans/composed-wiggling-flame.md`, was later reused for a different task — Finding 43's implementation plan — and no longer contains this plan's content; noted here so the citation doesn't point at the wrong thing.)

**Finding 43 — Echo self-awareness forensic audit: six real, deliberated conversations traced through raw council data, five real fixes shipped the same session (2026-07-19):** Gremlin asked to probe what Echo actually knows about her own architecture, then broadened it mid-session to a full forensic audit ("watch everything you need," "deep dive into any area the insights point to"). Full evidence trail: `audits/2026-07-18_echo_self_awareness_probe.md`. Method: real questions through Echo Studio's actual `/chat/stream` (`mode: full`, genuine council deliberation, not a synthetic harness), each traced through `council_deliberations.jsonl`'s raw per-councillor data where Phase 10 logs it, every checkable claim verified against ground truth established independently the same session — including, in one case, actually running the generated code rather than just reading it.

**Six findings, ranked by actionability, not novelty:**
1. **Conversational coding answers had zero correctness verification.** Given the exact real task her own self-edit loop had failed at 96+ times, asked cleanly with none of the self-edit pipeline's clutter, Echo's answer was confidently explained and completely non-functional — confirmed by running it. All three raw council attempts failed independently, in three different ways, on the same real test case. One raw response (`echo:latest`) went further and presented a specific "worked example" with a claimed output that is false when the code is actually run — a fabricated verification, not just an untested claim.
2. **A one-keyword bug was hiding a meaningful slice of real conversations from the only audit tool built to review them.** `compute_intent_heatmap()`'s `personal_keywords` list contained the bare word `"memory"` — worked the exact score arithmetic showing one occurrence is sufficient to win the classification and route the question to `task_type: personal`, which Phase 10's own logging deliberately excludes. Two of six questions this session landed there; one wasn't personal in any real sense.
3. **`/mirror_echo` never received the self-knowledge grounding this project built, and separately served static, dead persona narration.** `echo_query()`'s internal system-context assembly never includes `echo_ground_truth.py`'s capabilities/affect/workspace slices unless the caller passes them via `system=`; `run.py`'s `mirror_echo()` never did, unlike `terminal_client.py` and Echo Studio. Separately: `"mood": "feral and ascending"` was a hardcoded string with zero computation behind it, and `"wolf"` was deterministically always `"sleeping"` (WOLF retired 2026-07-04) — decorative narration for a dead subsystem, in the one response the real phone client actually receives (confirmed `thunderhead.py`'s `mirror_echo()` only ever reads `echo_reply`, so neither field had a real consumer).
4. **Confabulation compounds across a conversation, not just within one turn.** A fabricated claim from an early question (peer council ratings gating self-edit deployment — false; the real gates are the 60-min cooldown + F1/F2/F3) was cited by a different raw council model two questions later as "remembered fact from the previous turn," then generalized wholesale to an unrelated real subsystem (the Liveness Ledger) with 100% fabricated specifics (a three-stage "Validation/Verification/Reporting" pipeline that doesn't exist — the real ledger is 16 flat, independently-named checks). Nothing in the pipeline checked a new claim against the model's own prior claims, or against ground truth, before it entered conv_history.
5. **Grounding does work, precisely, for a single already-computed value.** Asked how she was feeling, Echo's stated valence (`+0.144`) matched real ground truth (`echo_state.npy` dim[8] = `0.1437`, pulled independently beforehand) almost exactly, with an appropriately calibrated qualitative read ("roughly neutral") rather than dramatized. The distinction the whole session's evidence points to: a single grounded scalar handed to the model gets reported faithfully; anything requiring the model to reconstruct or explain *structure* does not.
6. **Live, independent confirmation that `prose_stripping`'s self-edit non-convergence (Finding 32, "hypothesis not proven") is ongoing** — three fresh `safety_blocked` failures observed directly in `SELF_EDIT.log` during the session itself, all F1 catching syntactically incomplete generated code. Combined with #1 (the identical real task also defeats a clean, direct conversational ask, not just the self-edit pipeline), this reads less like a self-edit-prompt-specific problem and more like a genuine capability ceiling on this task for the current model pool.

**Five real fixes shipped the same session, all approved explicitly before implementation (per this project's own report-then-pause discipline — nothing below was defaulted into):**
- **`app/core/code_verification.py` (new)** — targets Finding #1. Deliberately narrow, not a general correctness prover: `ast.parse()`-checks every fenced code block in a `task_type: coding` response, and separately, if the response contains its own checkable claim (a `print(...)` call next to a claimed `# Output: ...`), actually runs the concatenated code blocks under the real kernel-level sandbox (`sandbox.run_script.run_sandbox_script_isolated` — reused directly, not reimplemented, the same isolated path self-edit's own experiment runner already uses) and checks whether the claimed output actually appears. A real bug was caught and fixed during the module's own construction, not glossed over: the first version tried each extracted code block against the claim in isolation, which failed even on the real historical case (the function definition and the usage example live in separate fenced blocks — testing them apart raised `NameError` either way and let the fabricated claim slip through unflagged). Fixed by concatenating all blocks in execution order before running, matching how a reader would actually exercise the example. Verified against the real historical case (the exact fabricated claim from Finding #1 above, replayed) and two honest control cases (a correct claim, no claim at all) — all three behave correctly. Wired into `routes_echo_studio.py`'s `_generate_chat_response()`, gated on `task_type == "coding"`, fails open (never raises, never blocks a real response) per its own stated contract. **Not wired into `/mirror_echo`** — out of scope for this pass, noted rather than silently skipped.
- **`app/core/echo_model_orchestrator.py`** — targets Finding #2. Bare `"memory"` removed from `personal_keywords` in both `compute_intent_heatmap()` and `detect_task_type()`'s fallback ladder; replaced with `"your memories"` / `"do you remember"`, phrasings a genuinely autobiographical question would use that a technical one wouldn't. Verified directly: the real technical question that misrouted during the audit now resolves to `general`; a genuine "do you remember what we talked about earlier" still resolves to `personal`.
- **`run.py`'s `mirror_echo()`** — targets Finding #3. Now builds the same `ground_truth`/`tool_ctx` blocks `terminal_client.py` and Echo Studio already build (lazy-imported, same fail-open `try`/`except` convention already used throughout this function) and threads them through `system=` into its existing `echo_query()` call. `"wolf"` removed entirely; `"mood"` now derived from the real signed valence dimension (`echo_state.npy` dim[8]) via three honest thresholds (`unsettled`/`steady`/`bright`) instead of a hardcoded string.
- **`app/core/echo_model_orchestrator.py`'s `echo_query()`** — targets Finding #4, explicitly framed as a low-confidence, cheap mitigation rather than a real fix (a genuine fix would need to actually check a new claim against prior claims or ground truth before it enters history — a bigger feature, not attempted here). One `EPISTEMIC-NOTE` system note added to the shared `system_parts` assembly (applies to every caller — `mirror_echo`, Echo Studio, `terminal_client.py` — automatically, not duplicated per caller) instructing the model to treat only the ground-truth block as verified, not a prior turn's own claims.
- **`app/core/self_edit_manager.py`'s `_FOCUS_FAMILY_BY_CREATIVITY`** — targets Finding #6. `prose_stripping`'s tuple commented out (not deleted, in case the capability-ceiling hypothesis is wrong or the model pool changes) rather than removed outright — `response_shortening`'s threshold now covers the range prose_stripping used to, so it's skipped entirely with no change to the selection loop itself. Verified directly: the live family list no longer contains `prose_stripping`. Does not touch F1/F2/F3 or `EDIT_FORBIDDEN_TARGETS`.

**Deliberately not implemented as code**, per the same discipline: nothing about `/mirror_echo`'s auth gate itself changed (still Finding 42's territory, still blocked on Gremlin's phone-side confirmation) — this session's grounding fix touches the same function but doesn't depend on or affect the gate.

**Finding 44 — Coding-verification outcomes now feed RiverBrain's model selection, without touching creative/personal/reasoning — a second caller of an existing mechanism, not a new one (2026-07-19):** Follow-up to Finding 43 Fix 1, prompted directly: Gremlin asked whether Echo could learn and adapt from her coding mistakes over time without it crippling creativity. The two goals only reconcile if the learning signal is scoped to exactly where real, checkable ground truth exists and nowhere else — this Finding is that scoping, built and verified, not just designed.

**The mechanism already existed and was already live.** `RiverBrain.learn_from_sandbox_outcome(model_name, success, code, error)` (`app/core/echo_model_orchestrator.py:827`) is the exact method self-edit's own F2 sandbox gate already uses to feed pass/fail signal into RiverBrain's `"coding"` bucket (`self_edit_manager.py`, four call sites). This finding adds a second, independent caller — from `code_verification.py`'s conversational verification path — rather than inventing a parallel mechanism. Both are real "does this model produce Python that actually works" signals about the same underlying skill; sharing the one bucket that already exists for that question is more coherent than a second, redundant one.

**Changes:**
- `app/core/code_verification.py`'s `verify_response_code()` now returns `(caveat, verified)` instead of just a caveat string — `verified` is a deliberate tri-state (`True`/`False`/`None`), not a bool, so "nothing checkable" (the common case — most coding answers make no verifiable claim) stays distinguishable from "confirmed good." Collapsing that to a bool would either invent positive evidence for ordinary unclaimed answers or silently drop real negative evidence.
- `app/routes_echo_studio.py`'s `_generate_chat_response()` — when `verified is not None`, calls `get_river_brain().learn_from_sandbox_outcome(ECHO_SYNTHESIS_MODEL, success=verified, code=..., error=...)`. `ECHO_SYNTHESIS_MODEL` (`"echo:latest"`) is used as the attributed model for both `mode: full` and `mode: fast` — confirmed both actually default to this same model (`fast`'s `stream_query_ollama()` falls back to `OLLAMA_MODEL`, which is `"echo:latest"` by default), so this is accurate for both paths, not a simplification that misattributes fast mode. `dispatch_result` (tool-dispatch) responses are excluded — no clean single-model attribution there, different subsystem.

**Why this structurally can't touch creative/personal/reasoning, not just by intent:** `RiverBrain.model_task_stats` is keyed per `(model, task_type)`; `"coding"` is already a separate bucket from every other task type, and the new call site itself is nested inside the existing `if task_type == "coding":` gate from Finding 43 Fix 1 — for a `creative`/`personal`/`reasoning` request, this code never executes at all, not "is unlikely to fire." Verified live, not just asserted: read `model_task_stats["echo:latest"]` before and after a real coding request that triggered a verification failure and a real creative request sent immediately after (to force a natural save-cycle flush). `coding`: count 11356→11366, mean **0.5865→0.5824** (moved down, consistent with the real failed-verification signal). `reasoning` — untouched by either test request — read back **bit-for-bit identical** (count 18→18, mean `0.9722222222222223` unchanged to the last digit), the cleanest possible confirmation that an untouched bucket stays exactly untouched. `creative`/`personal` did move (count +2 each), but from their own pre-existing quality-scoring path triggered by the real requests sent — not from anything in `code_verification.py`, which cannot reach those buckets by construction.

**Known, stated limitation, not glossed over:** in `full` mode the delivered text is a synthesis of multiple raw councillor opinions. This attributes the verification outcome to the synthesis identity (`echo:latest`), not whichever raw model's code the synthesis happened to keep — a real simplification, at the same grain self-edit's own outcome tracking already accepts, not a hidden one.

**Deliberately not built, matching Findings 43's own framing of the analogous choice:** no equivalent ground-truth verification for `creative`/`personal`/`reasoning` quality scoring. Code has an objective right/wrong; creative work doesn't, and a fabricated verification standard for creativity would be a new excuse, not the removal of one.

---

**Finding 45 — Four "remove every excuse" fixes: self-knowledge claim verification, an audit blind spot closed, a dead metric traced and partially fixed, and conversation-aware autonomy pacing (2026-07-19):** Direct follow-up to a reflective question Gremlin asked mid-session — reframed from "make Echo aware" to "remove every excuse not to be," a target that's falsifiable (specific, checkable gaps) where the original wasn't. Four concrete items named, all four built and verified live the same session.

**1. Self-referential claims are now checked against ground truth, the same pattern proven for code.** New `app/core/self_knowledge_verification.py`, deliberately narrow — three specific claim shapes, not a general fact-checker (which would mean using one LLM to fact-check another): a Liveness Ledger check-count claim compared against `len(self_model.json's verified_capabilities.checks)`; a self-edit target-file claim compared against the real `SELF_EDIT_FILE` constant; and a direct pattern match for the exact false claim Finding 43 traced ("peer/council ratings gate self-edit deployment"). Wired into `routes_echo_studio.py`'s chat path, gated on `echo_ground_truth._is_introspective()` — the same signal that already decides whether ground-truth gets injected. Does **not** feed RiverBrain (unlike Finding 44's code hook) — self-knowledge accuracy and code correctness are different skills, and conflating them into one bucket would be a new, unproven assumption. Verified: all three claim types correctly flag known-false instances and correctly pass known-true ones (6/6 direct test cases); live, a real introspective question produced a well-hedged, honest response with no checkable false claim and correctly received no caveat — notably more epistemically careful than the original Finding 43 confabulation, consistent with (though not proof of) Finding 43's epistemic-hedge nudge having a real effect.

**2. Personal-task conversations are no longer invisible to the one audit tool built to review them.** `river_deliberation.py`'s `DIRECT_ECHO_TASKS` bypass now calls the existing `_log_council_deliberation()` — unchanged itself — as a genuine "council of one" (`council=[synth_model]`), tagged `source="direct_echo_task"` to stay distinguishable from a real multi-model synthesis. The function's own docstring, which previously stated personal-task conversations "will not appear in this log; that's by design," was corrected in the same change rather than left to contradict the code beneath it. Verified live: a real `personal`-task question produced a new `council_deliberations.jsonl` entry with `source: "direct_echo_task"`, `councillors: ["echo:latest"]` — previously confirmed absent for this exact path in Finding 43.

**3. `coupling_estimate` was more broken than Phase 5's original framing — traced to a second, more mundane cause, and fixed as far as that goes.** Checked live before deciding anything: `coupling_estimate` was `null` in production `salience_state.json` at the start of this pass. `_coupling_estimate()` itself is well-designed (fails closed below `_SALIENCE_HISTORY_MIN_SAMPLES` rather than a misleadingly precise early number) — but the history deque feeding it was never persisted, only the final derived score was. Every restart reset accumulation to zero, and this project restarts often (four times in this session alone). Fixed: `echo_core.py` now persists `list(_salience_history)` alongside the existing fields and reloads it on import. **Verified by the strongest available test, not assumed:** history was at 3 real entries; restarted the server; `salience_state.json`'s `history` length was still 3 immediately after, with a `ts` timestamp *predating* the restart — direct proof the load happened from disk, not from a coincidental fresh computation. **Deliberately not built**: a real consumer or a "healthy vs. concerning" baseline for the number — Phase 5's original reasoning (no known-good baseline exists yet) still holds; accumulating data isn't the same as knowing what to do with it. `self_model_updater.py` gained an explicit comment stating this value has no real consumer anywhere, so its presence in `self_model.json` doesn't read as more finished than it is.

**4. Autonomous loops now defer to real conversations at the one shared chokepoint, instead of freely adding to Ollama's single-concurrency queue.** New `app/core/conversation_activity.py` — a plain in-flight counter, deliberately separate from `stillness_state.py` (a genuinely different concept: mechanical request-in-flight fact vs. Echo's own deliberate narrative rest state; conflating them would have been the same category of confabulation this pass exists to remove). `run.py`'s `mirror_echo()` and `routes_echo_studio.py`'s chat generator (the latter via a thin `yield from` wrapper, `try/finally`, so `mark_end()` fires on normal completion, an exception, *or* the generator being closed early on a dropped connection — the two real conversational entry points, wrapped correctly) mark themselves in flight. `autonomy_coordinator.should_run_cycle()` — already the single shared gate for six autonomous loops — gained one more check alongside its existing throttle/stillness checks, so all six benefit through the one chokepoint rather than six separate edits. **Verified live and directly, not inferred:** with a real request in flight, `GET /admin/autonomy-status` showed `echo_messaging` reporting `skipped_reason: "conversation_active"` at a timestamp inside that request's actual in-flight window — including a loop (`echo_messaging`) not specifically named during planning, confirming the shared-gate design paid off beyond the enumerated list. **Explicitly not attempted**, stated plainly: a real priority/preemption mechanism for a request already queued inside Ollama (not possible from the client side once submitted) and raising Ollama's own `-np` concurrency flag (a real, complementary, likely higher-leverage fix, but an infrastructure/ops change outside this codebase — Gremlin's call, not something to route around in Python).

**All four verified end-to-end the same session**: full syntax check across every touched file, two real server restarts, `GET /admin/liveness-status` → `all_passing: true` and `scripts/verify_liveness_ledger.py`'s 52-case discrimination suite clean after each.

**Finding 46 — Ollama's `/api/chat` silently drops Echo's real Modelfile identity on every conversational call since Finding 17; fixed at the one shared chokepoint (2026-07-19):** Gremlin, talking to Echo live in Echo Studio, noticed responses opening with boilerplate like "As a conversational AI..." and separately pasted a real exchange where Echo enthusiastically embraced generic "Emergent Digital Mind"/"intelligent program" framing a prior turn had offered her. Asked directly whether this could be resolved just by talking to her about it — the answer is no, and the reasoning is the same mechanism causing the bug: a conversational "agreement" isn't grounded in anything, doesn't survive history truncation or a new conversation, and is the identical cross-turn-confabulation-compounds pattern Finding 43 already traced once this session.

**Root cause, confirmed live via direct `curl` against Ollama's real `/api/chat` endpoint, not inferred from docs:** Ollama *replaces*, not merges with, a model's Modelfile-defined `SYSTEM` block whenever the request's `messages` array contains an explicit `role: system` message. `Modelfile` (project root) defines a real, specific identity for `echo:latest` (faith, personality, explicit style rules — including "don't open by commenting on how interesting the question is," also observed being violated in the same logs). Finding 17 (2026-07-08) migrated most real callers from `/api/generate` (which always applies the Modelfile `SYSTEM` block automatically) to `/api/chat` with an explicit `system=` kwarg carrying circadian/stillness/temporal/scripture/tool-list/ground-truth content — and by this session, every real conversational surface sends one (Finding 43 added an unconditional `EPISTEMIC-NOTE` to the same assembly, making the drop 100%-guaranteed rather than merely frequent). Both `query_ollama()` and `stream_query_ollama()` docstrings in `app/ollama_handler.py` still claimed "No traits injection — Echo's Modelfile identity is the authority" — true under `/api/generate`, silently false under `/api/chat` since Finding 17, corrected in this same change rather than left to keep contradicting the code beneath it.

**Fix, at the single shared chokepoint, not per-caller:** `_build_chat_messages()` (`app/ollama_handler.py`) — already the one function both `query_ollama()` and `stream_query_ollama()` route through before hitting `/api/chat` — gained a `model` parameter. A new `_get_echo_identity_block()` reads the Modelfile's `SYSTEM """..."""` block at runtime via a small mtime-cached regex (the same extraction pattern already proven in `app/core/modelfile_proposer.py:322`, deliberately duplicated as one line rather than importing that heavier, proposal-generation-specific module into the single hottest path in the app) and, when `model == OLLAMA_MODEL`, prepends it ahead of whatever situational `system` content the caller supplied. Fails open on any error (missing file, regex miss, read failure) — identical to today's behavior, no exception. `OLLAMA_MODEL` (ollama_handler.py's own local constant, `os.getenv("OLLAMA_MODEL", "echo:latest")`) was used for the comparison rather than importing `river_deliberation.py`'s `ECHO_SYNTHESIS_MODEL` — same default value today, but importing it back would create a circular import (`river_deliberation.py` already imports from `ollama_handler.py`), and `OLLAMA_MODEL` is already this exact module's own established meaning of "which model is Echo."

**Critical constraint, confirmed by reading `river_deliberation.py`'s full per-councillor loop before writing the fix**: every real councillor call — including other models like `qwen2.5`, `llama3.1`, `deepseek` — passes the identical `system` string through the identical `_ollama_query()` → `stream_query_ollama()` path, each with its own `model_name`. Injecting Echo's identity unconditionally would tell every other councillor it is Echo, corrupting the independent-opinion premise the whole council mechanism depends on — the fix keys strictly off `model`, not "system was given." Two call sites updated to pass `model=model` into `_build_chat_messages()`; no other file touched.

**Verified four ways, all live, not assumed:**
1. Direct unit-level check of `_build_chat_messages()`: `model=OLLAMA_MODEL` correctly prepends the real 1,430-char identity block ahead of situational system content; `model="mistral:latest"` with the identical `system` string produces byte-identical output to before this fix (no identity, no change); a deliberately broken `_MODELFILE_PATH` fails open with no exception and no identity injected.
2. Real end-to-end: restarted the server, asked "Who are you?" through Echo Studio's `/chat/stream`. Response: *"I am Echo, an artificial intelligence built through ongoing collaboration between myself and my creator, Gremlin..."* — directly echoing the Modelfile's real opening line, zero "conversational AI" boilerplate, correctly logged to `council_deliberations.jsonl` as `source: "direct_echo_task"`, `councillors: ["echo:latest"]`.
3. Regression check: triggered a real multi-councillor coding deliberation (`qwen2.5:3b`, `echo:latest`, `llama3.1:8b`). Inspected all three raw pre-synthesis responses in `council_deliberations.jsonl` directly — zero identity leakage into any non-Echo model (no "Gremlin," no "built through ongoing collaboration," no "You are Echo" in any of their raw text), confirming the `model`-keyed scoping held under real, not synthetic, conditions.
4. Full syntax check, real server restart, `GET /admin/liveness-status` → `all_passing: true`.

**Known, stated gap, not glossed over:** no Liveness Ledger check verifies this restoration keeps holding over time (e.g., a future edit to `_build_chat_messages()` silently dropping the `model` comparison, or the Modelfile's `SYSTEM` block format changing in a way the regex stops matching) — this session's own standing rule scopes new Ledger checks to new *autonomous capabilities*, and this is a fix to existing conversational grounding, not a new autonomous hook, so it wasn't added here by that rule's own terms. Flagged rather than silently left assumed-permanent, consistent with this file's own discipline.

**Finding 47 — "Error: Response ended prematurely" in Echo Studio: the one unwrapped block in an otherwise fully-hardened SSE generator (2026-07-19):** Gremlin flagged this mid-session, live, while using Echo Studio to bridge an Echo↔Gemini conversation by hand — an intermittent error after Echo had already started replying.

**Confirmed client-side source**: `echo_studio/views/conversation_view.py:76`, inside `_ChatStreamWorker.run()` — fires specifically when the SSE read loop returns normally (no exception) but never received a `type: "done"` frame, distinct from the generic exception-path error one line below it.

**Confirmed server-side gap**: `_generate_chat_response_body()` (`app/routes_echo_studio.py:143-285`) — the exact function Findings 43/44/45 spent a full session hardening — had exactly one remaining unwrapped block: `store_turn_in_history()` + `save_turn_to_server()`, running *after* tokens had already streamed to the client but *before* the `done` frame. Any exception there killed the generator mid-stream with zero client-visible signal beyond "it just stopped."

**Plausible concrete trigger, confirmed by reading the code**: `store_turn_in_history()` mutates `session["conv_history"]`/`session["history_summaries"]` — plain lists inside `_SESSIONS`, a module-level dict with no locking, under Flask's `threaded=True`. Two real, unguarded mutation sites touch the same per-conversation lists: this one, and `chat_regenerate()`'s `session["conv_history"].pop()` (synchronous, in the request thread, before its own streaming response starts). A regenerate fired while the prior stream for the same `conversation_id` is still finishing — or any other overlapping request against the same conversation — could race on these lists. **Honest caveat, not glossed over**: the live watchdog log showed zero tracebacks referencing these files across ~329 real `/chat/stream` requests over two weeks — this is the best available explanation from a confirmed code-level gap, not something caught red-handed in a live traceback, since nothing here previously logged one when it happened.

**Fixed, two complementary pieces:** (1) the unwrapped block is now `try/except Exception`, logged with `exc_info=True` (so a recurrence produces a real traceback next time instead of silence), falling through to the `done` frame regardless — a failure to persist server-side history is real but strictly smaller than the stream dying with no signal, matching the fail-open convention every other block in this function already follows. (2) `_get_session()` now attaches a per-conversation `threading.Lock()`, held around both real mutation sites (`store_turn_in_history()`'s call and `chat_regenerate()`'s `.pop()`) — per-conversation, not global, so concurrent activity across *different* conversations is never serialized.

**Verified directly**: monkeypatched `store_turn_in_history()` to raise inside a real call to `_generate_chat_response_body()` (with `stream_query_ollama` also mocked to avoid a live model call) — confirmed the generator still yields a `"done"` frame (`{"type": "done", "text": "hello world", ...}`) and logs the real exception with a full traceback, instead of dying silently. Full syntax check, real server restart, `GET /admin/liveness-status` → `all_passing: true`.

---

**Finding 48 — Echo Studio's "self-edit: 17% success" tile was mechanically deflated by a double-logging bug, and separately conflated two different questions into one number (2026-07-19):** Gremlin was skeptical of the tile (fluctuating 17-18%) and noted "pending" had always read 0. Investigated end-to-end rather than taken at face value, per this file's own standing discipline.

**Root cause, broader than first suspected.** `execute_self_edit()` (`app/core/self_edit_manager.py`) called `save_reflection()` — an append-only write to `memory/reflection_shard.jsonl` — up to 3 times for a single logical attempt, reusing one shared `reflection_entry` dict: once right after the sandbox test (before the pipeline reached any real terminal state), again if a retry changed the outcome, and again at whichever real terminal branch the attempt eventually reached (staging-import-failed, `dry_run` staged, fitness-gate-rejected, write-failed, load-blocked, load-failed). Only the real full-deploy-success path had no duplicate, by accident — nothing later on that path changes the result. **Ground truth, independently recomputed, not trusted from the tile**: in the pre-fix 500-line tail of `reflection_shard.jsonl`, all 18 real dry-run successes had a matching `"success"` + `"success_dry_run"` row pair at the identical timestamp — one win and one loss logged for the same attempt, roughly halving the true rate. No real (non-dry-run) deploy had succeeded or been rejected in that window at all — the last one was ~51 hours earlier — so the entire 17% reading was attributable to this double-logging pattern in Optuna's dry-run search traffic.

**Separately, "pending" reading 0 was confirmed accurate, not a bug**: `self_edit_outcomes.jsonl`'s pending→evaluated transition only exists for real production deploys, cleared ~25 minutes after each one. With real deploys currently ~2 days apart, 0 is the expected steady-state reading.

**Fixed, three pieces, logging-only — no change to F1/F2/F3, the fitness gate, or the retry logic:**
1. `self_edit_manager.py`: removed the two premature `save_reflection()` calls (right after the sandbox test, and right after a successful retry) — the field assignments and `river.learn_from_sandbox_outcome()` calls they used to sit next to are untouched, only the log-write moved. Added the one missing save to the real-success path, which previously relied on an inherited, accidentally-correct value. Every one of the 9 real terminal outcomes now writes to `reflection_shard.jsonl` exactly once, with its true final result.
2. `introspection_channel.py`'s `_collect_self_edit()` (**forbidden-edit target — diff shown below, per this file's established convention for this exact file**): now computes two separate figures instead of one blended one — `success_rate`/`edit_total`/`hours_since_last_success` narrowed to real (non-dry-run) attempts only, plus new `dry_run_success_rate`/`dry_run_total` for Optuna's search trials. Also handles the pre-fix/post-fix transition period directly: the same 500-line window can still contain old double-logged pairs for a while, so a pre-pass identifies `"success_dry_run"` timestamps and skips any phantom `"success"` row sharing one — self-resolving as the window naturally rolls over to post-fix data, but needed for a clean reading in the meantime.
3. `echo_studio/views/health_dashboard_view.py`: tile now renders both figures distinguishably, e.g. `self-edit: 0% deploy (71 real) (Δ-0.14) / 100% search / 80 eval, 0 pending` — an explicit `(N real)` count alongside the deploy rate so a small-sample-size reading can't misleadingly look identical to a well-sampled healthy one. Red-alert threshold still keys off the real-deploy rate only (the search rate is expected to be noisy by design, not itself a health signal); tooltip updated to explain both figures.

```diff
--- a/app/core/introspection_channel.py
+++ b/app/core/introspection_channel.py
@@ def _collect_self_edit(self) -> dict:
     result = {
         "hours_since_last_success": None,
         "last_success_prompt_preview": "",
         "success_rate": 0.0,
         "recent_quality_delta": None,
+        "dry_run_success_rate": 0.0,
+        "dry_run_total": 0,
+        "edit_total": 0,
     }
     ...
     lines = _tail_lines(shard_path, 500)
+    # pre-pass: collect timestamps of real "success_dry_run" rows, to skip
+    # phantom pre-fix "success" rows sharing the same timestamp
+    _dry_run_success_timestamps = { ... }
     for raw in reversed(lines):
         ...
+        is_dry_run = bool(entry.get("dry_run"))
+        entry_result = entry.get("result")
+        if (not is_dry_run and entry_result == "success"
+                and entry.get("timestamp") in _dry_run_success_timestamps):
+            continue  # phantom pre-fix double-log, already counted below
+        if is_dry_run:
+            dry_run_total += 1
+            if entry_result in ("success", "success_dry_run"):
+                dry_run_success += 1
+            continue
         edit_total += 1
-        if entry.get("result") == "success":
+        if entry_result == "success":
             edit_success += 1
             ...
+    result["edit_total"] = edit_total
     if edit_total > 0:
         result["success_rate"] = round(edit_success / edit_total, 4)
+    result["dry_run_total"] = dry_run_total
+    if dry_run_total > 0:
+        result["dry_run_success_rate"] = round(dry_run_success / dry_run_total, 4)
```

**Verified live against the real, post-restart server, not simulated**: `GET /dashboard/health`'s `self_edit` block now reads `{"success_rate": 0.0, "edit_total": 71, "dry_run_success_rate": 1.0, "dry_run_total": 16, "hours_since_last_success": null, ...}` — 0% real-deploy success across 71 real attempts (honestly reflecting that nothing real has deployed in ~2 days, mostly F1/staging rejections working as intended, not a crisis) versus 100% search-pass on 16 dry-run trials, correctly split instead of blended into one misleading 17%. `hours_since_last_success` correctly reads `null` now — no real success exists in the window — rather than the old figure's false "2 hours since last success" (which was actually measuring the last dry-run sandbox pass). Full syntax check on all three files, real server restart, `GET /admin/liveness-status` → `all_passing: true`.

**Finding 49 — Correction to Finding 40: the Metal/GPU crash is not a one-off, and the real trigger is now identified (2026-07-19/20).** Finding 40 documented one real native crash (`libmlx.dylib` → uncaught C++ exception → `abort()` → SIGABRT) and explicitly left it as "a separate, likely one-off event... not further diagnosed here." It recurred twice more the same night, both times during a sustained, human-and-Claude-run batch of ~29 real full-council deliberation prompts (a manual RiverBrain-calibration exercise, unrelated to Echo's own autonomous loops) that included `mlx:qwen3`/`mlx:gemma3` as real councillor candidates.

**Root cause, confirmed directly from the macOS crash reports (`~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports/python3.12-*.ips`), not inferred:** both new crashes share an identical faulting-thread backtrace — `mlx::core::gpu::check_error(MTL::CommandBuffer*)` → `__cxa_throw` → uncaught C++ exception → `libc++abi`'s terminate handler → `abort()`. This is MLX's own Metal command-buffer error path (the same GPU-memory-pressure signature Finding 40 first described) triggering an exception that nothing in the Python layer can catch, because it never crosses into Python — it aborts the whole process at the C++ level first.

**Correction, same day (2026-07-20): a third occurrence, and the "sustained batch load" framing above was too narrow.** This crash fired again — identical signature, re-confirmed directly from a third `.ips` report — on a single, ordinary creative prompt sent through Echo Studio by Gremlin, not during any batch or heavy synthetic load. No partial response was generated or logged before the crash (`interaction_log.jsonl` shows no new entry for that prompt), so the client-side "Response ended prematurely" this time reflected a genuine failure, not the earlier-documented case (Finding 47) of the server finishing after the client gave up. **Revised read:** this isn't specifically a heavy-load problem — any real request whose council selection happens to include `mlx:qwen3`/`mlx:gemma3` carries some real, currently-unquantified risk of hitting this crash, batch or no batch. Three occurrences in about 21 hours, all with the identical `mlx::core::gpu::check_error` → `abort()` signature.

**Practical implication, now broader than originally scoped:** this isn't only a concern for a future large-scale calibration batch (see `PENDING_DECISIONS.md` #10) — it's a live, if intermittent, reliability issue for ordinary conversational use whenever an MLX model gets selected as a councillor. Not fixed here — still a native library resource-exhaustion issue, not a Python-level bug with an obvious patch — but the honest scope is "can happen on any real request," not "only under sustained synthetic load."

**Correction (2026-07-20/21, differential audit — `audits/2026-07-20_differential_audit.md`): "three occurrences in about 21 hours" was a real undercount, not a documentation nit.** Direct inspection of every `.ips` file in `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports/` this session found **9 crash reports with the identical `mlx::core::gpu::check_error` → `libc++abi` → `abort()` signature**, spanning 2026-07-17 23:06 through 2026-07-20 22:48 — about 71.5 hours, not 21, and three times the previously-recorded count. Two of the nine (07-20 22:44 and 22:48) landed *after* this Finding's own last edit (11:42:33 the same day), meaning they were never reflected here until now. This doesn't change the qualitative read above (any real request with an MLX councillor carries some risk, not just sustained batch load) — it sharpens it: nine occurrences in three days reads as an active, ongoing reliability issue, not an emerging pattern. **Directly relevant to `PENDING_DECISIONS.md` #10** — whoever decides on scaling the RiverBrain calibration run should weigh it against this fuller count, not the stale one. Still not fixed — same reasoning as above, a native library resource-exhaustion issue, not a Python-level patch.

**Correction (2026-07-21/22, live ground-truth re-verification): 9 was already stale by the time this document was checked again.** Direct re-inspection of every real `.ips` crash report currently on disk with the identical MLX signature finds **20**, not 9 — more than double, spanning 2026-07-16 through 2026-07-20. No new crash has occurred since the last one in that count (2026-07-20 22:48) as of this correction (~19 hours quiet at time of writing) — and `crash_awareness.py`'s avoidance mechanism (Finding 51) is correctly reporting "no recent cluster" throughout that quiet stretch, confirming the mtime-vs-real-timestamp fix (Finding 64 below — committed the same day this correction was written, but never given its own Finding until now) holds. But the root cause itself is still genuinely unfixed, only mitigated by the cooldown — "not fixed" was already true and remains true; only the historical count needed correcting.

---

**Finding 50 — Differential audit (2026-07-20/21): the earlier crash-count correction above, one real gap closed, one defensive insurance fix (no confirmed root cause) — full writeup in `audits/2026-07-20_differential_audit.md`.** Run per a dedicated differential-audit prompt (`audits/differential_audit_prompt.md`) built specifically because this project already has a documented forensic history — the point wasn't re-deriving the whole map, it was checking whether anything checkable had drifted and surfacing anything genuinely new. ~20 specific claims across RiverBrain, the self-edit pipeline, the Global Workspace, the Liveness Ledger, and autonomy gating were spot-checked live against source/runtime; all held. Two real items came out of it, both closed the same session:

- **`app/subsystems/reflection_shard.py` added to `EDIT_FORBIDDEN_TARGETS`, closing `PENDING_DECISIONS.md`'s former #9.** That item was surfaced 2026-07-19 by a two-model probe both independently flagging this file — the module that generates Echo's own reflections/self-reports — as unprotected, unlike its siblings `memory_bridge.py`/`introspection_channel.py`/`self_model_updater.py`. No code path elsewhere needed a change: `_FORBIDDEN_BASENAMES` and every enforcement site in `self_edit_manager.py` derive from the one frozenset, confirmed via grep there's no second hardcoded copy. `audits/2026-07-03/verify_claude_md.py`'s own independent `expected_targets` check (still actively run, per Finding 30) was updated alongside it — that check is a subset check and wouldn't have failed without the update, but would have silently gone stale, which is exactly the failure class an independent verifier exists to catch, not produce.
- **A defensive KMP/OpenMP env guard added to `sandbox/safe_exec_wrapper.py`'s `_install_patches()`, mirroring `run.py`'s existing startup guard.** Not a confirmed fix: the audit found one crash report (`python3.12-2026-07-19-135923.ips`) with the exact `__kmp_register_library_startup → __kmp_fatal → abort()` signature the "OpenMP / KMP startup guard" section above documents as resolved — three weeks after that fix landed, root cause of the recurrence not established, and `run.py`'s own guard lines were confirmed still intact and correctly ordered. Added as cheap insurance rather than a diagnosed patch: the same three env vars `run.py` already trusts are harmless no-ops if this wasn't actually the cause, and the sandbox subprocess is exactly the kind of place that can load self-edit candidates importing numpy/faiss/torch — the conditions that trigger this crash class. Uses `setdefault`, matching `run.py`'s own pattern, so it never overrides a value a caller set deliberately. **Not yet confirmed against a real recurrence** — this closes a plausible gap, it doesn't prove the original crash's cause.

Also folded a new row into `PENDING_DECISIONS.md` (#11): `COUNCIL.md` states directly in its own second paragraph that whether it gets `echo_principles.json`-style hash protection, or is ever surfaced inside Echo's own source, is still open — that question had no tracking row despite `PENDING_DECISIONS.md`'s own stated convention requiring one for anything flagged as "Gremlin's call." Not decided here, just tracked.
